[^n890]And that man from whom the devils went out entreated that he might stay with him; but [4] Jesus sent him away, and said unto him, [^n891]Return to thy house, and make known what [5] God hath done for thee. [^n892]And he went, and began to publish in Decapolis[^n893] what Jesus had done for him; and they all marvelled.

[6] [^n894]And when Jesus had crossed in the ship to that side, a great multitude received [7] him; and they were all looking for him. [^n895]And a man named Jairus, the chief of the [8] synagogue, fell before the feet of Jesus, [^n896]and besought him much, and said unto him, I have an only daughter, and she is come nigh unto death; [^n897]but come and lay thy [9] hand upon her, and she shall live. [^n898]And Jesus rose, and his disciples, and they followed [10] him. [^n899]And there joined him a great multitude, and they pressed him.

[11, 12] [^n900]And a woman, which had a flow of blood for twelve years, [^n901]had suffered much of many physicians, and spent all that she had, and was not benefited at all, but her [13] trouble increased further. [^n902]And when she heard of Jesus, she came in the thronging of [14] [Arabic, p. 47] the crowd behind him, and touched[^n903] his garments; [^n904]and she thought within [15] herself, If I could reach to touch his garments, I should live. [^n905]And immediately the fountain of her blood was dried; and she felt in her body that she was healed [16] of her plague. [^n906]And Jesus straightway knew within himself that power had gone out of him; and he turned to the crowd, and said, Who approached unto my garments? [17] [^n907]And on their denying, all of them, Simon Cephas and those with him said unto him, Our Master, the multitudes throng thee and press thee, and sayest thou, Who approached [18] unto me? [^n908]And he said, Some one approached unto me; and I knew that [19] power went forth from me. [^n909]And that woman, when she saw that she was not hid [20] from him, came fearing and agitated (for she knew what had happened to her), [^n910]and fell down and worshipped him, and told, in the presence of all the people, for what [21] reason she touched him, and how she was healed immediately. [^n911]And Jesus said unto her, Be of good courage, daughter; thy faith hath made thee alive; depart in peace, and be whole from thy plague.

[22] [^n912]And while he was yet speaking, there came a man from the house of the chief of the synagogue, and said unto him, Thy daughter hath died; so trouble not the [23] teacher. [^n913]But Jesus heard, and said unto the father of the maid, Fear not: but believe [24] only, and she shall live. [^n914]And he suffered no man to go with him, except [25] Simon Cephas, and James, and John the brother of James. [^n915]And they reached the house of the chief of the synagogue; and he saw them agitated, weeping and wailing. [26] [^n916]And he entered, and said unto them, Why are ye agitated and weeping? the [27] [Arabic, p. 48] maid hath not died, but she is sleeping. [^n917]And they laughed at him, for [28] they knew that she had died. [^n918]And he put every man forth without, and took the father of the maid, and her mother, and Simon, and James, and John, and [29] entered into the place where the maid was laid. [^n919]And he took hold of the hand of the maid, and said unto her, Maid, arise. [^n920]And her spirit returned, and straightway [30] she arose and walked: [^n921]and she was about twelve years of age. [^n922]And he commanded [31] that there should be given to her something to eat. [^n923]And her father wondered greatly: [32] and he warned them that they should tell no man what had happened. [^n924]And this report spread in all that land.

[33] [^n925]And when Jesus crossed over from there, there joined him two blind men, crying [34] out, and saying, Have mercy on us, thou son of David. [^n926]And when he came to the house, those two blind men came to him: and Jesus said unto them, Believe ye [35] that I am able to do this? They said unto him, Yea, our Lord. [^n927]Then he touched[^n928] [36] their eyes, and said, As ye have believed, it shall be unto you. [^n929]And immediately their eyes were opened. And Jesus forbade them, and said, See that no man know. [37] [^n930]But they went out and published the news in all that land.

[38] [^n931]And when Jesus went out, they brought to him a dumb man having a devil. [39] [^n932]And on the going out of the devil that dumb man spake. And the multitudes marvelled, and said, It was never so seen in Israel

[40] [^n933]And Jesus was going about in all the cities and in the villages, and teaching in their synagogues, and proclaiming the good news of the kingdom, and healing every disease [41] [Arabic, p. 49] and sickness; and many followed him. [^n934]And when Jesus saw the multitudes, he had compassion on them, for they were wearied and scattered,[^n935] as sheep [42] that have no shepherd. [^n936]And he called his twelve disciples, and gave them power and [43] much authority over all devils and diseases; [^n937]and sent them two and two, that they [44] might proclaim the kingdom of God, and to heal the sick. [^n938]And he charged them, and said, Walk not in the way of the heathen, nor enter into the cities of the Samaritans.[^n939] [45, 46] [^n940]Go especially unto the sheep that are lost of the sons of Israel. [^n941]And [47] when ye go, proclaim and say, The kingdom of heaven is come near. [^n942]And heal the sick, and cleanse the lepers, and cast out the devils: freely ye have received, freely [48, 49] give. [^n943]Get you not gold, nor silver, nor brass in your purses; [^n944]and take nothing for the way, except a staff only; nor bag, nor bread; neither shall ye have two tunics, [50] nor shoes, nor staff, but be shod with sandals; [^n945]for the labourer is worthy of his food. [51] [^n946]And whatever city or village ye enter, inquire who is worthy in it, and there be until [52, 53] ye go out. [^n947]And when ye enter into the house, ask for the peace of the house: and if the house is worthy, your peace shall come upon it; [^n948]but if it is not worthy, your [54] peace shall return unto you. [^n949]And whosoever shall not receive you, nor hear your sayings, when ye go out from that house, or from that village, shake off the dust [55] [Arabic, p. 50] that is under your feet against them for a testimony. [^n950]And verily I say unto you, To the land of Sodom and Gomorrah there shall be rest in the day of judgement, rather than to that city.

### Section XIII

Section XIII.

[1] [^n951]I am sending you as lambs among wolves: be ye now wise as serpents, and [2] harmless[^n952] as doves. [^n953]Beware of men: they shall deliver you to the councils of the [3] magistrates, and scourge you in their synagogues; [^n954]and shall bring you before governors and kings for my sake, for a testimony against them and against the nations. [4] [^n955]And when they deliver you up, be not[^n956] anxious, nor consider beforehand, what ye [5] shall say; but ye shall be given[^n957] in that hour what ye ought to speak. [^n958]Ye do not [6] speak, but the Spirit of your Father speaketh in you. [^n959]The brother shall deliver up his brother to death, and the father his son; and the sons shall rise against their [7] parents, and put them to death. [^n960]And ye shall be hated of every man because of [8] my name; but he that endureth unto the end of the matter shall be saved.[^n961] [^n962]When they expel you from this city, flee to another. Verily I say unto you, Ye shall not finish all the cities of the people of Israel, until the Son of man come.

[9, 10] [^n963]A disciple is not superior to his lord, nor a servant to his master. [^n964]For it is enough then for the disciple that he be as his lord, and the servant as his master. If they have called the master of the house Beelzebul, how much more the people [11] of his house! [^n965]Fear them not therefore:[^n966] for there is nothing covered, that shall [12] [Arabic, p. 51] not be revealed; nor hid, that shall not be disclosed and published. [^n967]What I say unto you in the darkness, speak ye in the light; and what ye have told [13] secretly in the ears in closets, let it be proclaimed on the housetops. [^n968]I say unto you now, my beloved, Be not agitated at[^n969] those who kill the body, but have no power to [14] kill the soul. I will inform you whom ye shall fear: him[^n970] which is able to destroy [15] soul and body in hell. [^n971]Yea, I say unto you, Be afraid of him especially. Are not two sparrows sold for a farthing in a bond?[^n972] and one of them shall not fall on the [16] ground without your Father. [^n973]But what concerns you: even the hair of your heads [17, 18] also is numbered. [^n974]Fear not therefore; ye are better than many sparrows. [^n975]Every man who confesseth me now before men, I also will confess him before my Father [19] which is in heaven; [^n976]but whosoever denieth me before men, I also will deny him before my Father which is in heaven.

[20] [^n977]Think ye that I am come to cast peace into the earth? I came not to cast peace, [21] but to cast dissension. [^n978]Henceforth there shall be five in one house, three of them [22] disagreeing with two, and the two with the three. [^n979]The father shall become hostile to his son, and the son to his father; and the mother to her daughter, and the daughter to her mother; and the mother in law to her daughter in law, and the daughter [23] in law to her mother in law: [^n980]and a man’s enemies shall be the people of his house. [24] [^n981]Whosoever loveth father or mother better than me is not worthy of me; and whosoever [Arabic, p. 52] loveth son or daughter more than his love of me is not worthy of me. [25] [^n982]And every one that doth not take his cross and follow me is not worthy of [26] me. [^n983]Whosoever findeth his life[^n984] shall lose it; and whosoever loseth his life[^n985] for my sake shall find it.

[27] [^n986]And whosoever receiveth you receiveth me; and whosoever receiveth me receiveth [28] him that sent me. [^n987]And whosoever receiveth a prophet in the name of a prophet shall take[^n988] a prophet’s reward; and whosoever shall receive a righteous man [29] in the name of a righteous man shall take[^n989] a righteous man’s reward. [^n990]And every one that shall give to drink to one of these least ones a drink of water only, in the name of a disciple, verily I say unto you, he shall not lose his reward.

[30] [^n991]And when Jesus finished charging his twelve disciples, he removed thence to [31] teach and preach in their cities. [^n992]And while they were going in the way they entered into a certain village; and a woman named Martha entertained him in her house. [32] [^n993]And she had a sister named Mary, and she came and sat at the feet of our Lord, [33] and heard his sayings. [^n994]But Martha was disquieted by much serving; and she came and said unto him, My Lord, givest thou no heed that my sister left me alone to [34] serve? speak to her that she help me. [^n995]Jesus answered and said unto her, Martha, [35] Martha, thou art solicitous and impatient[^n996] on account of many things: [^n997]but what is sought is one thing. But Mary hath chosen for herself a good portion, and that which shall not be taken from her.

[36] [^n998]And the apostles went forth, and preached to the people that they might repent. [37] [^n999]And they cast out many devils, and anointed many sick with oil, and healed them. [38, 39] [^n1000]And the disciples of John told him[^n1001] of all these things. [^n1002]And when John heard in [Arabic, p. 53] the prison of the doings of the Messiah, he called two of his disciples, and sent them to Jesus, and said, Art thou he that cometh, or look we for [40] another? [^n1003]And they came to Jesus, and said unto him, John the Baptist hath sent [41] us unto thee, and said, Art thou he that cometh, or look we for another? [^n1004]And in that hour he cured many of diseases, and of plagues of an evil spirit; and he gave sight [42] to many blind. [^n1005]Jesus answered and said unto them, Go and tell John everything ye have seen and heard: the blind see, and the lame walk, and the lepers are cleansed, and the blind[^n1006] hear, and the dead rise, and the poor have the gospel preached to [43] them. [^n1007]And blessed is he who doubteth not in me.

[44] [^n1008]And when John’s disciples departed, Jesus began to say to the multitudes concerning John, What went ye out into the wilderness to see? a reed shaken with the [45] winds? [^n1009]And if not, then what went ye out to see? a man clothed in soft raiment? Behold, they that are in magnificent garments and in voluptuousness are in the abode [46] of kings. [^n1010]And if not, then what went ye out to see? a prophet? Yea, I say unto [47] you, and more than a prophet. [^n1011]This is he of whom it is written,

I am sending my messenger before thy face

To prepare the way before thee.

### Section XIV

Section XIV.

[1] [^n1012]Verily I say unto you, There hath not arisen among those whom women have borne a greater than John the Baptist; but he that is little now in the kingdom of heaven is greater than he.

[2] [Arabic, p. 54] [^n1013]And all the people which heard, and the publicans, justified[^n1014] God, for [3] they had been baptized with the baptism of John. [^n1015]But the Pharisees and the scribes wronged[^n1016] the purpose of God in themselves, in that they were not baptized of [4] him. [^n1017]And from the days of John the Baptist until now the kingdom of heaven is [5] snatched away by violence. [^n1018]The law and the prophets were until John; and after that, the kingdom of God is preached, and all press to enter it: [^n1019]and they that exert themselves [6, 7] snatch it away. [^n1020]All the prophets and the law until John prophesied. [^n1021]And if ye [8] will, then receive it, that he is Elijah, which is to come. [^n1022]Whosoever hath ears that hear [9] let him hear. [^n1023]Easier is the perishing of heaven and earth, than the passing away of [10] one point of the law. [^n1024]To whom then shall I liken the people of this generation,[^n1025] and [11] to whom are they like? [^n1026]They are like the children sitting in the market, which call to their companions, and say, We sang to you, and ye danced not; we wailed to you, [12] and ye wept not. [^n1027]John the Baptist came neither eating bread nor drinking wine; [13] and ye said, He hath demons: [^n1028]and the Son of man came eating and drinking; and ye said, Behold, a gluttonous man, and a drinker of wine, and an associate of publicans [14, 15] and sinners! [^n1029]And wisdom was justified of all her children. [^n1030]And when he said that, they came to the house. And there gathered unto him again multitudes, [16] so that they found not bread to eat. [^n1031]And while he was casting out a devil which was dumb, when he cast out that devil, that dumb man spake. And the multitudes [17] [Arabic, p. 55] marvelled. [^n1032]And the Pharisees, when they heard, said, This man doth not cast out the devils, except by Beelzebul the chief of the demons, which is in him. [18, 19] [^n1033]And others requested of him a sign from heaven, to tempt him. [^n1034]And Jesus knew their thoughts, and said unto them in parables, Every kingdom that withstandeth itself shall become desolate; and every house or city that disagreeth with itself shall not [20] stand: [^n1035]and if a devil cast out a devil, he withstandeth himself; neither shall he be [21] able to stand, but his end shall be. [^n1036]Then how now shall his kingdom stand? for ye [22] said that I cast out devils by Beelzebul. [^n1037]And if I by Beelzebul cast out the devils, then your children, by what do they cast them out? And for this cause they shall [23] be judges against you. [^n1038]But if I by the Spirit of God cast out devils, then the kingdom [24] of God is come near unto you. [^n1039]Or how can a man enter into the house of a valiant man, and seize his garments,[^n1040] if he do not beforehand secure himself[^n1041] from [25] that valiant man? and then will he cut off[^n1042] his house. [^n1043]But when the valiant man is [26] armed, guarding his house, his possessions are in peace. [^n1044]But if one come who is more valiant than he, he overcometh him, and taketh his whole armour, on which [27] he relieth, and divideth his spoil. [^n1045]Whosoever is not with me is against me; and [28] whosoever gathereth not with me scattereth abroad. [^n1046]For this reason I say unto you, [Arabic, p. 56] that all sins and blasphemies with which men blaspheme shall be forgiven [29] them: [^n1047]but whosoever shall blaspheme against the Holy Spirit, there is no [30] forgiveness for him for ever, but he is deserving of eternal punishment: [^n1048]because they [31] said that he had an unclean spirit. [^n1049]And he said also, Every one that speaketh a word against the Son of man, it shall be forgiven him; but whosoever speaketh against the Holy Spirit, it shall not be forgiven him, neither in this world, nor in the world to [32] come. [^n1050]Either ye must make a good tree[^n1051] and its fruit good; or ye must make an evil [33] tree[^n1052] and its fruit evil: for the tree is known by its fruit. [^n1053]Ye children of vipers, how can ye, being evil, speak good things? from the overflowings of the heart the mouth [34] speaketh. [^n1054]The good man from the good treasures which are in his heart bringeth forth good things; and the wicked man from the evil treasures which are in his [35] heart bringeth forth evils. [^n1055]I say unto you, that every idle word which men shall [36] speak, they shall give an answer for in the day of judgement: [^n1056]for by thy sayings thou shalt be justified, and by thy sayings thou shalt be judged.

[37] [^n1057]And he said to the multitudes, When ye see the clouds appear from the west, [38] straightway ye say that there cometh rain; and so it cometh to pass. [^n1058]And when [39] the south wind bloweth, ye say that there will be heat; and it cometh to pass. [^n1059]And when the evening is come, ye say, It will be fair weather, for the heaven has become [40] red. [^n1060]And in the morning ye say, To-day there will be severe weather, for the redness [Arabic, p. 57] of the heaven is paling. [^n1061]Ye hypocrites, ye know to examine the face of the heaven and the earth; but the signs of this time ye know not to discern.

[41] [^n1062]Then they brought to him one possessed of a demon, dumb and blind; and he [42] healed him, so that the dumb and blind began to speak and see. [^n1063]And all the multitudes wondered, and said, Is this, think you, the son of David?

[43] [^n1064]And the apostles returned unto Jesus, and told him everything that they had [44] done and wrought.[^n1065] [^n1066]And he said unto them, Come, let us go into the desert alone, and rest ye a little. And many were going and returning, and they had not leisure, not even to eat bread.

[45] [^n1067]And after that, there came to him one of the Pharisees, and besought him that he would eat bread with him. And he entered into the house of that Pharisee, and [46] reclined. [^n1068]And there was in that city a woman that was a sinner; and when she knew that he was sitting in the house of that Pharisee, she took a box of sweet ointment, [47] and stood behind him, [^n1069]towards his feet, weeping, and began to wet his feet with her tears, and to wipe them with the hair of her head, and to kiss his feet, and [48] anoint them with the sweet ointment. [^n1070]And when that[^n1071] Pharisee saw it, who invited him, he thought within himself, and said, This man, if he were a prophet, would know who she is and what is her history: for the woman which touched him was a sinner.

### Section XV

Section XV.

[1] [^n1072]Jesus answered and said unto him, Simon, I have something to say unto thee. And [2] he said unto him, Say on, my Master. [^n1073]Jesus said unto him, There were two debtors [Arabic, p. 58] to one creditor; and one of them owed five hundred pence, and the other [3] owed fifty pence. [^n1074]And because they had not wherewith to pay, he forgave [4] them both. Which of them ought to love him more? [^n1075]Simon answered and said, I suppose, he to whom he forgave most. Jesus said unto him, Thou hast judged rightly. [5] [^n1076]And he turned to that woman, and said to Simon, Dost thou see this woman? I entered into thy dwelling, and thou gavest me not water to wash my feet: but this [6] woman hath bathed[^n1077] my feet with her tears, and dried them with her hair. [^n1078]And thou kissedst me not: but this woman, since she[^n1079] entered, hath not ceased to kiss my [7] feet. [^n1080]And thou anointedst not my head with oil:[^n1081] but this woman hath anointed [8] my feet with sweet ointment.[^n1082] [^n1083]And for this, I say unto thee, Her many sins are forgiven her, because she loved much; for he to whom little is forgiven loveth little. [9, 10] [^n1084]And he said unto that woman, Thy sins are forgiven thee. [^n1085]And those that were invited [11] began to say within themselves, Who is this that forgiveth sins also? [^n1086]And Jesus said to that woman, Thy faith hath saved thee; go in peace.

[12] [^n1087]And many believed in him when they saw the signs which he was doing. [13, 14] [^n1088]But Jesus did not trust[^n1089] himself to them, [^n1090]for he knew every man, and he needed not any man to testify to him concerning every man; for he knew what was in man.

[15] [^n1091]And after that, Jesus set apart from his disciples other seventy, and sent them two and two before his face to every region and city whither he was purposing to [16] go. [^n1092]And he said unto them, The harvest is abundant, and the labourers are few: [17] entreat now the Lord of the harvest, that he send forth labourers into his harvest. [^n1093]Go [18] [Arabic, p. 59] ye: and lo, I am sending you as lambs among wolves. [^n1094]Take not with you [19] purses, nor a wallet, nor shoes; neither salute any man in the way. [^n1095]And [20] whatsoever house ye enter, first salute that house: [^n1096]and if there be there a son of peace, [21] let your peace rest upon him; but if there be not, your peace shall return to you. [^n1097]And be ye in that house eating and drinking what they have:[^n1098] for the labourer is worthy of [22] his hire. [^n1099]And remove not from house to house. And into whatsoever city ye enter, [23] and they receive you, eat what is presented to you: [^n1100]and heal the sick that are [24] therein, and say unto them, The kingdom of God is come near unto you. [^n1101]But whatsoever city ye enter, and they receive you not, go out into the market, and say, [25] [^n1102]Even the dust that clave to our feet from your city, we shake off against you; but [26] know[^n1103] this,[^n1104] that the kingdom of God is come near unto you. [^n1105]I say unto you, that for Sodom there shall be quiet in the day of judgement, but there shall not be for [27] that city. [^n1106]Then began Jesus to rebuke the cities in which there had been many [28] mighty works,[^n1107] and they repented not. [^n1108]And he said, Woe unto thee, Chorazin! woe unto thee, Bethsaida! if there had been in Tyre and Sidon the signs which were in [29] thee, it may be that they would have repented in sackcloth and ashes. [^n1109]Howbeit I say unto you, that for Tyre and Sidon there shall be rest in the day of judgement, [30] more than for you. [^n1110]And thou, Capernaum, which art exalted unto heaven, shalt sink down unto Hades; for if there had been in Sodom the wonders[^n1111] which were [31] in thee, it would have remained until this day. [^n1112]And now I say unto thee, that for the land of Sodom there shall be quiet in the day of judgement, more than for thee.

[32] [Arabic, p. 60] [^n1113]And he said again unto his apostles, Whosoever heareth you heareth me; and whosoever heareth me heareth him that sent me: and whosoever wrongeth[^n1114] you wrongeth me; and whosoever wrongeth me wrongeth him that sent me.

[33] [^n1115]And those seventy returned with great joy, and said unto him, Our Lord, even [34] the devils also are subject unto us in thy name. [^n1116]He said unto them, I beheld [35] Satan[^n1117] fallen like lightning from heaven. [^n1118]Behold, I am giving you authority to tread upon serpents and scorpions, and the whole race[^n1119] of the enemy; and nothing shall [36] hurt you. [^n1120]Only ye must not rejoice that the devils are subject unto you; but be glad that your names are written in heaven.

[37] [^n1121]And in that hour Jesus rejoiced in the Holy Spirit, and said, I acknowledge thee, my Father, Lord of heaven and earth, that thou didst hide these things from the wise and understanding, and didst reveal them unto children: yea, my Father; so [38] was thy will. [^n1122]And he turned to his disciples,[^n1123] and said unto them, Everything hath been delivered to me of my Father: and no man knoweth who the Son is, save the Father; and who the Father is, save the Son, and to whomsoever the Son willeth [39] to reveal him. [^n1124]Come unto me, all of you, ye that are wearied and bearers of burdens, [40] and I will give you rest. [^n1125]Bear my yoke upon you, and learn of me; for[^n1126] I [41] am gentle and lowly in my heart: and ye shall find rest unto your souls. [^n1127]For my yoke is pleasant, and my burden is light.

[42] [^n1128]And while great multitudes were going with him, he turned, and said unto them, [43] [^n1129]Whosoever cometh unto me, and hateth not his father, and his mother, and his brethren, and his sisters, and his wife, and his children, and himself[^n1130] also, cannot [44] [Arabic, p. 61] be a disciple to me. [^n1131]And whosoever doth not take his cross, and follow [45] me, cannot be a disciple to me. [^n1132]Which of you desireth to build a tower, and doth not sit down first and reckon his expenses and whether he hath enough to [46] complete it?[^n1133] [^n1134]lest when he hath laid the foundations, and is not able to finish, all that [47] behold him[^n1135] laugh at him, and say, [^n1136]This man began to build, and was not able to [48] finish. [^n1137]Or what king goeth to the battle to fight with another king,[^n1138] and doth not consider first whether he is able with ten thousand to meet him that cometh to him [49] with twenty thousand? [^n1139]And if he is not able, he sendeth unto him while he is afar [50] off, and seeketh peace. [^n1140]So shall[^n1141] every man of you consider, that desireth to be a disciple to me; for if he renounceth not all that he hath, he cannot be a disciple to me.

### Section XVI

Section XVI.

[1] [^n1142]Then answered certain of the scribes and Pharisees, that they might tempt him, [2] and said, Teacher, we desire to see a sign from thee. [^n1143]He answered and said, This evil and adulterous generation[^n1144] seeketh a sign; and it shall not be given a sign, [3] except the sign of Jonah the prophet. [^n1145]And as Jonah was a sign to the inhabitants [4] of Nineveh, so shall the Son of man also be to this generation. [^n1146]And as Jonah was in the belly of the great fish three days and three nights, so shall the Son of man [5] be in the heart of the earth three days and three nights. [^n1147]The queen of the south shall rise in the judgement with the people of this generation, and condemn[^n1148] them: for she came from the ends of the earth that she might hear the wisdom of Solomon; [6] [Arabic, p. 62] and behold, here is a better than Solomon. [^n1149]The men of Nineveh shall stand in the judgement with this generation, and condemn it: for they repented at [7] the preaching of Jonah; and behold, here is a greater than Jonah. [^n1150]The unclean spirit, when he goeth out of the man, departeth, and goeth about through places wherein are no waters, that he may find rest for himself; and when he findeth it not, he [8] saith, I will return to my house whence I came out. [^n1151]And if he come and find it [9] adorned and set in order, [^n1152]then he goeth, and associateth with himself seven other spirits worse than himself; and they enter and dwell in it: and the end of that man [10] shall be worse than his beginning. [^n1153]Thus shall it be unto this evil generation.

[11] [^n1154]And while he was saying that, a woman from the multitude lifted up her voice, and said unto him, Blessed is the womb that bare thee, and the breasts that nursed [12] thee. [^n1155]But he said unto her, Blessed is he that heareth the word of God, and keepeth it.

[13] [^n1156]And while he was speaking unto the multitude, there came unto him his mother [14] and his brethren, and sought to speak with him; [^n1157]and they were not able, because of [15] the multitude; and they stood without and sent, calling him unto them. [^n1158]A man said unto him, Behold, thy mother and thy brethren are standing without, and seek to [16] speak with thee. [^n1159]But he answered unto him that spake unto him, Who is my [17] mother? and who are my brethren? [^n1160]And he beckoned with his hand, stretching it out towards his disciples, and said, Behold, my mother! and behold, my brethren! [18] [^n1161]And every man that shall do the will of my Father which is in heaven is my brother, and my sister, and my mother.

[19] [^n1162]And after that, Jesus was going about in the cities and in the villages, and proclaiming [Arabic, p. 63] and preaching the kingdom of God, and his[^n1163] twelve with him, [20] [^n1164]and the women which had been healed of diseases and of evil spirits, Mary [21] that was called Magdalene, from whom he had cast out seven devils, [^n1165]and Joanna the wife of Chuza Herod’s steward, and Susanna, and many others, who were ministering to them of their substance.

[22] [^n1166]And after that, Jesus went out of the house, and sat on the sea shore. [^n1167]And there [23] gathered unto him great multitudes. And when the press of the people was great upon him, he went up and sat in the boat; and all the multitude was standing on the [24] shore of the sea. [^n1168]And he spake to them much in parables, and said, The sower [25] went forth to sow: and when he sowed, [^n1169]some fell on the beaten highway; and it was [26] trodden upon, and the birds ate it. [^n1170]And other fell on the rocks: and some, where there was not much earth; and straightway it sprang up, because it had no depth in [27] the earth: [^n1171]and when the sun rose, it withered; and because it had no root, it dried [28] up. [^n1172]And some fell among thorns; and the thorns sprang up with it, and choked it; [29] and it yielded no fruit. [^n1173]And other fell into excellent and good[^n1174] ground; and it came up, and grew, and brought forth fruit, some thirty, and some sixty, and some [30] a hundred. [^n1175]And when he said that, he cried, He that hath ears that hear, let him [31] hear. [^n1176]And when they were alone, his disciples came, and asked him, and said unto [32] him, What is this parable? and why spakest thou unto them in parables? [^n1177]He [Arabic, p. 64] answered and said unto them, Unto you is given the knowledge of the secrets of the kingdom of God; but it is not given unto them that are [33] without. [^n1178]He that hath shall be given unto, and there shall be added; and he that [34] hath not, that which he hath shall be taken from him also. [^n1179]For this cause therefore I speak unto them in parables; because they see, and see not; and hear, and hear [35] not, nor understand. [^n1180]And in them is being fulfilled the prophecy of Isaiah, who said,

Hearing they shall hear, and shall not understand;

And seeing they shall see, and shall not perceive:

[36] [^n1181]The heart of this people is waxed gross,

And their hearing with their ears is become heavy,

And they have closed their eyes;

Lest they should see with their eyes,

And hear with their ears,

And understand with their hearts,

And should return,

And I should heal them.

[37, 38] [^n1182]But ye, blessed are your eyes, which see; and your ears, which hear. [^n1183]Blessed [39] are the eyes which see what ye see. [^n1184]Verily I say unto you, Many of the prophets and the righteous longed to see what ye see, and saw not; and to hear what ye [40] hear, and heard not. [^n1185]When ye know not this parable, how shall ye know all parables? [41, 42] [^n1186]Hear ye the parable of the sower. [^n1187]The sower which sowed, sowed the word [43] of God. [^n1188]Every one who heareth the word of the kingdom, and understandeth it not, the evil one cometh and snatcheth away the word that hath been sown in his [44] heart: and this is that which was sown on the middle of the highway. [^n1189]But that which was sown on the rocks is he that heareth the word, and straightway receiveth [45, 46] [Arabic, p. 65] it with joy; [^n1190]only, it hath no root in his soul, but his belief in it is for a time; [^n1191]and whenever there is distress or persecution because of a[^n1192] word, he [47] stumbleth[^n1193] quickly. [^n1194]And that which was sown among the thorns is he that heareth the word; [^n1195]and the care of this world, and the error of riches, and the rest of the [48] other lusts enter, and choke the word, and it becometh without fruit. [^n1196]And that which was sown in good ground is he that heareth my word in a pure and good heart, and understandeth it, and holdeth to it, and bringeth forth fruit with patience, [^n1197]and produceth either a hundredfold or sixtyfold or thirty.

[49] [^n1198]And he said, So is the kingdom of God, like a man who casteth seed into the [50] earth, and sleepeth and riseth by night and day, [^n1199]and the seed groweth and cometh [51] up, whence[^n1200] he knoweth not. [^n1201]And the earth bringeth it to the fruit; and first it [52] will be blade, and after it ear, and at last perfect wheat in the ear: [^n1202]and whenever the fruit ripeneth,[^n1203] he bringeth immediately the sickle, for the harvest hath come.

### Section XVII

Section XVII.

[1] [^n1204]And he set forth to them another parable, and said, The kingdom of heaven is [2] like a man who sowed good seed in his field; [^n1205]but when men slept, his enemy came [3] and sowed tares among the wheat, and went away. [^n1206]And when the blade sprang up [4] and brought forth fruit, there were noticed the tares also. [^n1207]And the servants of the master of the house came, and said unto him, Our lord, didst thou not sow good [5] [Arabic, p. 66] seed in thy field? whence are there tares in it? [^n1208]He said unto them, An enemy hath done this. His servants said unto him, Wilt thou that we go [6] and separate it? [^n1209]He said unto them, Perhaps,[^n1210] when ye separate the tares, ye would [7] root up with them wheat also. [^n1211]Leave them to grow both together until the harvest: and in the time of the harvest I will say unto the reapers, Separate the tares first, and bind them in bundles to be burned with fire; and gather the wheat into my barns.

[8, 9] [^n1212]And he set forth to them another parable, and said, [^n1213]To what is the kingdom of [10] God like? and to what shall I liken it? and in what parable shall I set it forth? [^n1214]It [11] is like a grain of mustard seed, which a man took, and planted in his field: [^n1215]and of the number of the things that are sown in the earth it is smaller than all of the things [12] which are sown, which are upon the earth; [^n1216]but when it is grown, it is greater than all the herbs, and produceth large branches, so that the birds of heaven make their nests in its branches.

[13, 14] [^n1217]And he set forth to them another parable: [^n1218]To what shall I liken the kingdom of [15] God? [^n1219]It is like the leaven which a woman took, and kneaded into three measures of flour, until the whole of it was leavened.

[16] [^n1220]And Jesus spake all that to the multitudes by way of parables, according as they [17] were able to hear. [^n1221]And without parables spake he not unto them; that the saying of the Lord through the prophet might be fulfilled:

I will open my mouth in parables;

And I will utter secrets which were before the foundations[^n1222] of the world.

[18] [^n1223]But he explained to his disciples privately everything.

[19] [^n1224]Then Jesus left[^n1225] the multitudes, and came to the house. And his disciples came unto him, and said unto him, Explain unto us that parable about the tares [20] [Arabic, p. 67] and the field. [^n1226]He answered and said unto them, He that sowed good seed is [21] the Son of man; [^n1227]and the field is the world; and the good seed are the children of the [22] kingdom; [^n1228]and the tares are the children of the evil one; and the enemy that sowed them[^n1229] is Satan; and the harvest is the end of the world; and the reapers are the angels. [23] [^n1230]And as the tares are separated and burned in the fire, so shall it be in the end of [24] this world. [^n1231]The Son of man shall send his angels, and separate from his kingdom [25] all things that injure, [^n1232]and all the doers of iniquity, and they shall cast them into the [26] furnace of fire: and there shall be weeping and gnashing of teeth. [^n1233]Then the righteous shall shine as the sun in the kingdom of their Father. Whosoever hath ears that hear, let him hear.

[27] [^n1234]And again the kingdom of heaven is like treasure hid in a field: that which a man found and hid; and, for his pleasure in it, went and sold all that he had, and bought that field.

[28] [^n1235]And again the kingdom of heaven is like a man that is a merchant seeking excellent [29] pearls; [^n1236]and when he found one pearl of great price, he went and sold everything that he had, and bought it.

[30] [^n1237]And again the kingdom of heaven is like a net that was cast[^n1238] into the sea, and [31] gathered of every kind: [^n1239]and when it was filled, they drew it up on to the shore of the sea, and sat down to select; and the good of them they threw into the vessels, [32] and the bad they threw outside. [^n1240]Thus shall it be in the end of the world: the angels [33] shall go forth, [^n1241]and separate the wicked from among the good, and shall cast them into the furnace of fire: there shall be weeping and gnashing of teeth.

[34] [^n1242]Jesus said unto them, Have ye understood all these things? They said unto [35] [Arabic, p. 68] him, Yea, our Lord. [^n1243]He said unto them, Therefore every scribe that becometh a disciple of the kingdom of heaven is like a man that is a householder, who bringeth out of his treasures the new and the old.

[36, 37] [^n1244]And when Jesus had finished all these parables, he removed thence, and came to his city; [^n1245]and he taught them in their synagogues, so that they were perplexed. [38] [^n1246]And when the sabbath came, Jesus began to teach in the synagogue; and many of [39] those that heard marvelled, and said, Whence came these things to this man? And many envied him and gave no heed to him, but said, What is this wisdom that is given to this man, that there should happen at his hands such as these mighty works?[^n1247] [40] [^n1248]Is not this a carpenter, son of a carpenter? and is not his mother called Mary? and [41] his brethren, James, and Joses, and Simon, and Judas? [^n1249]And his sisters, all of them, [42] lo, are they not all with us? [^n1250]Whence hath this man all these things? And they were in doubt concerning him. [^n1251]And Jesus knew their opinion, and said unto them, Will ye haply[^n1252] say unto me this proverb, Physician, heal first thyself: and all that [43] we have heard that thou didst in Capernaum, do here also in thine own city? [^n1253]And he said, Verily I say unto you, A prophet is not received in his own city, nor among [44] his brethren: [^n1254]for a prophet is not despised, save in his own city, and among his own [45] kin, and in his own house. [^n1255]Verily I say unto you, In the days of Elijah the prophet, there were many widows among the children of Israel, when the heaven held back [46] three years and six months, [^n1256]and there was a great famine in all the land; and Elijah [Arabic, p. 69] was not sent to one of them, save to Zarephath of Sidon, to a woman that was [47] a widow. [^n1257]And many lepers were among the children of Israel in the days of Elisha the prophet; but not one of them was cleansed, save Naaman the Nabathæan.[^n1258] [48] [^n1259]And he was not able to do there many mighty works,[^n1260] because of their unbelief; [49] except that he laid his hand upon a few of the sick, and healed them. [^n1261]And he marvelled [50] at their lack of faith. [^n1262]And when those who were in the synagogue heard, [51] they were all filled with wrath; and they rose up, [^n1263]and brought him forth outside the city, and brought him to the brow of the hill upon which their city was built, that [52] they might cast him from its summit: [^n1264]but he passed through among them and went away.

[53] [^n1265]And he went about in the villages which were around Nazareth, and taught in their synagogues.

### Section XVIII

Section XVIII.

[1] [^n1266]At that time Herod the tetrarch heard of the fame of Jesus, and all the things which came to pass at his hand; [^n1267]and he marvelled, for he had obtained excellent [2] information concerning him.[^n1268] [^n1269]And some men said that John the Baptist was risen [3] from among the dead; [^n1270]and[^n1271] others said that Elijah had appeared; and others, Jeremiah; [4] and others, that a prophet of the old prophets was risen; [^n1272]and others said that he [5] was a prophet like one of the prophets. [^n1273]Herod said to his servants, This is John the Baptist, he whom I beheaded; he is risen from among the dead: therefore mighty [6] [Arabic, p. 70] works result from him. [^n1274]For Herod himself had sent and taken John, and cast him into prison, for the sake of Herodias his brother Philip’s wife, whom he [7] had taken. [^n1275]And John said to Herod, Thou hast no authority to take the wife of thy [8] brother. [^n1276]And Herodias avoided him and wished to kill him; and she could not. [9] [^n1277]But Herod feared John, for he knew that he was a righteous man and a holy; and [10] he guarded him, and heard him much, and did, and obeyed him with gladness. [^n1278]And he wished to kill him; but he feared the people, for they adhered to him as the [11] prophet. [^n1279]And there was a celebrated day, and Herod had made a feast for his great men on the day of his anniversary,[^n1280] and for the officers and for the chief men [12] of Galilee. [^n1281]And the daughter of Herodias came in and danced in the midst of the company, and pleased Herod and those that sat with him. And the king said to the [13] damsel, Ask of me what thou wilt, and I will give it thee. [^n1282]And he sware unto her, [14] Whatsoever thou shalt ask, I will give it thee, to the half of my kingdom. [^n1283]And she went out, and said unto her mother, What shall I ask him?[^n1284] She said unto her, The [15] head of John the Baptist. [^n1285]And immediately she came in hastily to the king, and said unto him, I desire in this hour that thou give me on a dish the head of John [16] the Baptist. [^n1286]And the king was exceeding sorry; but because of the oath and the [17] guests he did not wish to refuse her. [^n1287]But immediately the king sent an executioner, and commanded that he should bring the head of John: and he went and cut off [18] the head of John in the prison, [^n1288]and brought it on a dish, and delivered it to the [19] damsel; and the damsel gave it to her mother. [^n1289]And his disciples heard, and came [Arabic, p. 71] and took his body, and buried it. [^n1290]And they came and told[^n1291] Jesus what [20] had happened. [^n1292]And for this cause Herod said, I beheaded John: who [21] is this, of whom I hear these things. And he desired to see him. [^n1293]And Jesus, when he heard, removed thence in a boat to a waste place alone, to the other side of the sea of the Galilee of Tiberias.[^n1294]

[22] [^n1295]And many saw them going, and knew them, and hastened by land[^n1296] from all the cities, and came thither beforehand; [^n1297]for they saw the signs which he was doing on the [23, 24] sick. [^n1298]And Jesus went up into the mountain, and sat there with his disciples. [^n1299]And [25] the feast of the passover of the Jews was near. [^n1300]And Jesus lifted up his eyes, and saw great multitudes coming to him. [^n1301]And he was moved with compassion for them, for [26] they were like sheep that were without a shepherd. [^n1302]And he received them, and spake to them concerning the kingdom of God, and healed those who had need of healing.

[27] [^n1303]And when the evening approached,[^n1304] his disciples came to him, and said unto [28] him, [^n1305]The place is desert, and the time is past; send away the multitudes of the people,[^n1306] that they may go to the towns and villages which are around us, and buy for [29] themselves bread; for they have nothing to eat. [^n1307]But he said unto them, They have [30] no need to go away; give ye them what may be eaten. [^n1308]They said unto him, We have not here enough. [^n1309]He said unto Philip, Whence shall we buy bread that these may eat? [31, 32] [^n1310]And he said that proving him; and he knew what he was resolved to do. [^n1311]Philip said [Arabic, p. 72] unto him, Two hundred pennyworth of bread would not suffice them after[^n1312] [33] every one of them hath taken a small amount. [^n1313]One of his disciples said unto [34] him (namely, Andrew the brother of Simon Cephas), [^n1314]Here is a lad having five loaves [35] of barley and two fishes: but this amount, what is it for all these? [^n1315]But wilt thou that we go and buy for all the people what may be eaten? for we have no more [36] than these five loaves and the two fishes. [^n1316]And the grass was plentiful in that place. Jesus said unto them, Arrange all the people that they may sit down on the grass, [37] fifty people in a company. [^n1317]And the disciples did so. And all the people sat down [38] by companies, by hundreds and fifties. [^n1318]Then Jesus said unto them, Bring hither [39] those five loaves and the two fishes. [^n1319]And when they brought him that, Jesus took the bread and the fish, and looked to heaven, and blessed, and divided, and gave to [40] his disciples to set before them; [^n1320]and the disciples set for the multitudes the bread [41] and the fish; and they ate, all of them, and were satisfied. [^n1321]And when they were satisfied, he said unto his disciples, Gather the fragments that remain over, that nothing [42] be lost. [^n1322]And they gathered, and filled twelve baskets with fragments, being those that remained over from those which ate of the five barley loaves and the two [43] fishes. [^n1323]And those people who ate were five thousand, besides the women and children. [44] [Arabic, p. 73] [^n1324]And straightway he pressed his disciples to go up into the ship, and that they should go before him unto the other side to Bethsaida, while he [45] himself should send away the multitudes. [^n1325]And those people who saw the sign which [46] Jesus did, said, Of a truth this is a prophet who hath come into the world. [^n1326]And Jesus knew their purpose to come and take him, and make him a king; and he left them, and went up into the mountain alone for prayer.

[47, 48] [^n1327]And when the nightfall was near, his disciples went down unto the sea, and sat[^n1328] in a boat, and came to the side of Capernaum. [^n1329]And the darkness came on, and Jesus [49] had not come to them. [^n1330]And the sea was stirred up against them by reason of a violent [50] wind that blew. [^n1331]And the boat was distant from the land many furlongs, and they were much damaged by the waves, and the wind was against them.

### Section XIX

Section XIX.

[1] [^n1332]And in the fourth watch of the night Jesus came unto them, walking upon the [2] [^n1333]water, after they had rowed[^n1334] with difficulty about twenty-five or thirty furlongs. [3] [^n1335]And when he drew near unto their boat, his disciples saw him walking on the water; and they were troubled, and supposed that it was a false appearance; and they cried [4] out from their fear. [^n1336]But Jesus straightway spoke unto them, and said, Take courage, [5] for it is I; fear not. [^n1337]Then Cephas answered and said unto him, My Lord, if it be thou, [6] bid me to come unto thee on the water. [^n1338]And Jesus said unto him, Come. And [7] Cephas went down out of the boat, and walked on the water to come unto Jesus. [^n1339]But [Arabic, p. 74] when he saw the wind strong, he feared, and was on the point of sinking; [8] and he lifted up his voice, and said, My Lord, save me. [^n1340]And immediately our Lord stretched out his hand and took hold of him, and said unto him, [9] Thou of little faith, why didst thou doubt? [^n1341]And when Jesus came near, he went up [10] unto them into the boat, he and Simon, and immediately the wind ceased. [^n1342]And those that were in the ship came and worshipped him, and said, Truly thou art the [11] Son of God. [^n1343]And straightway that ship arrived at the land which they made for. [12] [^n1344]And when they came out of the ship to the land, they marvelled greatly and were [13] perplexed in themselves: [^n1345]and they had not understood by means of[^n1346] that bread, because their heart was gross.

[14] [^n1347]And when the people of that region knew of the arrival of Jesus, they made haste in all that land, and began to bring those that were diseased,[^n1348] borne in their [15] beds to the place where they heard that he was. [^n1349]And wheresoever the place might be which he entered, of the villages or the cities, they laid the sick in the markets, and sought of him that they might touch[^n1350] were it only the edge of his garment: and all that touched[^n1351] him were healed and lived.[^n1352]

[16] [^n1353]And on the day after that, the multitude which was standing on the shore of the sea saw that there was there no other ship save that into which the disciples had [17] gone up, [^n1354]and that Jesus went not up into the ship with his disciples (but there were other ships from Tiberias near[^n1355] the place where they ate the bread when Jesus blessed [18] it): [^n1356]and when that multitude saw that Jesus was not there, nor yet his disciples, they [19] [Arabic, p. 75] went up into those ships, and came to Capernaum, and sought Jesus. [^n1357]And when they found him on the other side of the sea, they said unto him, Our [20] Master, when camest thou hither? [^n1358]Jesus answered and said unto them, Verily, verily, I say unto you, Ye have not sought me because of[^n1359] your seeing the signs, but because of [21] your eating the bread and being satisfied. [^n1360]Serve not the food which perisheth, but the food which abideth in eternal life,[^n1361] which the Son of man will give unto you: him[^n1362] [22] hath God the Father sealed. [^n1363]They said unto him, What shall we do that we may [23] work the work of God? [^n1364]Jesus answered and said unto them, This is the work of [24] God, that ye believe in him whom he hath sent. [^n1365]They said unto him, What sign hast thou done, that we may see, and believe in thee? what hast thou wrought? [25] [^n1366]Our fathers ate the manna in the wilderness; as it was written, Bread from heaven [26] gave he them to eat. [^n1367]Jesus said unto them, Verily, verily, I say unto you, Moses gave you not bread from [27] heaven; but my Father gave[^n1368] you the bread of truth[^n1369] from heaven. [^n1370]The bread of God is that which came down from heaven and gave the [28, 29] world life. [^n1371]They said unto him, Our Lord, give us at all times this bread. [^n1372]Jesus said unto them, I am the bread of life: whosoever cometh unto me shall not hunger, [30] and whosoever believeth in me shall not thirst for ever. [^n1373]But I said unto you, [31] Ye have seen me, and have not believed. [^n1374]And all that my Father hath given to me cometh unto me; and whosoever cometh unto me I shall not cast him forth without. [32] [^n1375]I came down from heaven, not to do my own will, but to do the will of him [33] that sent me; [^n1376]and this is the will of him that sent me, that I should lose nothing of [34] [Arabic, p. 76] that which he gave me, but raise it up in the last day. [^n1377]This is the will of my Father, that every one that seeth the Son, and believeth in him, should have eternal life; and I will raise him up in the last day.

[35] [^n1378]The Jews therefore murmured against him because of his saying, I am the bread [36] which came down from heaven. [^n1379]And they said, Is not this Jesus, the son of Joseph, whose father and mother we know? then how saith this man, I came down from [37, 38] heaven? [^n1380]Jesus answered and said unto them, Murmur not one with another. [^n1381]No man is able to come unto me, except the Father which sent me draw him; and I will [39] raise him up in the last day. [^n1382]It is written in the prophet, They shall all be the taught of God. Every one who heareth from the Father now,[^n1383] and learneth of him, cometh [40] unto me. [^n1384]No man now seeth the Father; but he that is from God, he it is that seeth [41] the Father. [^n1385]Verily, verily, I say unto you, Whosoever believeth in me hath eternal [42, 43] life. [^n1386]I am the bread of life. [^n1387]Your fathers ate the manna in the wilderness, and [44] they died. [^n1388]This is the bread which came down from heaven, that a man may eat [45] of it, and not die. [^n1389]I am the bread of life which came down from heaven: and if a man eat of this bread he shall live for ever: [^n1390]and the bread which I shall give is my body, which I give for the life of the world.

[46] [^n1391]The Jews therefore quarrelled one with another, and said, How can he give us [47] [Arabic, p. 77] his body that we may eat it? [^n1392]Jesus said unto them, Verily, verily, I say unto you, If ye do not eat the body of the Son of man and drink his blood, ye shall [48] not have life in yourselves. [^n1393]Whosoever eateth of my body and drinketh of my blood [49] hath eternal life; and I will raise him up in the last day. [^n1394]My body truly is meat,[^n1395] and [50] my blood truly is drink.[^n1396] [^n1397]Whosoever eateth my body and drinketh my blood abideth [51] in me, and I in him—[^n1398]as the living Father sent me, and I am alive because of the [52] Father; and whosoever eateth me, he also shall live because of me. [^n1399]This is the bread which came down from heaven: and not according as your fathers ate the [53] manna, and died: whosoever eateth of this bread shall live for ever. [^n1400]This he said in [54] the synagogue, when he was teaching in Capernaum. [^n1401]And many of his disciples, when they heard, said, This word is hard; who is he that can hear it?

### Section XX

Section XX.

[1] [^n1402]And Jesus knew within himself that his disciples were murmuring because of [2] that, and he said unto them, Doth this trouble you? [^n1403]What if ye should see the Son [3] of man then ascend to the place where he was of old? [^n1404]It is the spirit that quickeneth, and the body profiteth nothing: the words[^n1405] that I speak unto you are spirit [4] and life. [^n1406]But there are some of you that do not believe. And Jesus knew beforehand who they were who should[^n1407] not believe, and who it was that should betray [5] him. [^n1408]And he said unto them, Therefore I said unto you, No man can come unto me, if that hath not been given him by the Father.

[6] [Arabic, p. 78] [^n1409]And because of this word many of his disciples turned back and walked [7] not with him. [^n1410]And Jesus said unto the twelve, Do ye haply also wish to [8] go away? [^n1411]Simon Cephas answered and said, My Lord, to whom shall we go? thou [9] hast the words of eternal life. [12] [^n1416]And while he was speaking, one of the Pharisees came asking of him that he [13] would eat with him: and he went in, and reclined to meat. [^n1417]And that Pharisee, when [14] he saw it,[^n1418] marvelled that he had not first cleansed himself before his eating. [^n1419]Jesus said unto him, Now do ye Pharisees wash the outside of the cup and the dish, and ye think that ye are cleansed; but your inside is full of injustice and wickedness.

[15, 16] [^n1420]Ye of little mind, did not he that made the outside make the inside? [^n1421]Now give what ye have[^n1422] in alms, and everything shall be clean unto you.

[17, 18] [^n1423]And there came to him Pharisees and scribes, come from Jerusalem. [^n1424]And when they saw some of his disciples eating bread while they had not washed their hands, [19] they found fault. [^n1425]For all of the Jews and the Pharisees, if they wash not their [20] hands thoroughly, eat not; for they held[^n1426] to the ordinance[^n1427] of the elders. [^n1428]And they ate not what was bought from the market, except they washed it; and many other things did they keep of what they had received, such as the washing of cups, and [21] measures, and vessels of brass, and couches. [^n1429]And scribes[^n1430] and Pharisees asked him, [Arabic, p. 79] Why do thy disciples not walk according to the ordinances of the elders, but [22] eat bread without washing their hands? [^n1431]Jesus answered and said unto them, Why do ye also overstep the command of God by reason of your ordinance? [23] [^n1432]God said, Honour thy father and thy mother; and, Whosoever revileth his father and [24] his mother shall surely die. [^n1433]But ye say, If a man say to his father or to his mother, [25] What thou receivest[^n1434] from me is an offering,—[^n1435]and ye[^n1436] suffer him not to do anything [26] for his father or his mother; [^n1437]and ye[^n1438] make void and reject the word of God by reason of the ordinance that ye have ordained and commanded, such as the washing [27] of cups and measures, and what resembles that ye do much. [^n1439]And ye forsook [28] the command of God, and held to the ordinance of men. [^n1440]Do[^n1441] ye well to wrong[^n1442] [29] the command of God in order that ye may establish your ordinance? [^n1443]Ye hypocrites, well did Isaiah the prophet prophesy concerning you, and say,

[30] [^n1444]This people honoureth me with its[^n1445] lips;

But their heart is very far from me.

[31] [^n1446]But in vain do they fear me,

In that they teach the commands of men.

[32] [^n1447]And Jesus called all the multitude, and said unto them, Hear me, all of you, and [33] understand: [^n1448]nothing without the man, which then enters him, is able to defile him; [34] but what goeth out of him, that it is which defileth the man. [^n1449]He that hath ears [35] that hear, let him hear. [^n1450]Then his disciples drew near, and said unto him, Knowest [36] thou that the Pharisees which heard this word were angry? [^n1451]He answered and said unto them, Every plant which my Father which is in heaven planted not shall be [37] [Arabic, p. 80] uprooted. [^n1452]Let them alone; for they are blind leading blind. And if the blind lead[^n1453] the blind, both of them shall fall into a hollow.

[38] [^n1454]And when Jesus entered the house from the multitude, Simon Cephas asked him, [39] and said unto him, My Lord, explain to us that parable. [^n1455]He said unto them, Do ye also thus not understand? Know ye not that everything that entereth into the [40] man from without cannot defile him; [^n1456]because it entereth not into his heart; it entereth into his stomach only, and thence is cast forth in the cleansing which maketh [41] clean all the food?[^n1457] [^n1458]The thing which goeth forth from the mouth of the man proceedeth [42] from his heart, and it is that which defileth the man. [^n1459]From within[^n1460] the [43] heart of men proceed evil thoughts, [^n1461]fornication, adultery, theft, false witness, murder, injustice, wickedness, deceit, stupidity, evil eye, calumny, pride, foolishness: [44] [^n1462]these evils all of them from within proceed from the heart, and they are the things [45] which defile the man: [^n1463]but if a man eat while he washeth not his hands, he is not defiled.

[46] [^n1464]And Jesus went out thence, and came to the borders of Tyre and Sidon. And he entered into a certain house, and desired that no man should know it;[^n1465] and [47] he could not be hid. [^n1466]But straightway a Canaanitish woman, whose daughter had an [48, 49] unclean spirit, heard of him. [^n1467]And that woman was a Gentile of Emesa of Syria. [^n1468]And she came out after him, crying out, and saying, Have mercy upon me, my Lord, thou [50] son of David; for my daughter is seized in an evil way by Satan.[^n1469] [^n1470]And he answered [Arabic, p. 81] her not a word. And his disciples came and besought him, and said, Send [51] her away: for she crieth after us. [^n1471]He answered and said unto them, I was [52] not sent except to the sheep that are gone astray of the house of Israel. [^n1472]But she came and worshipped him, and said, My Lord, help me, have mercy upon me.

[53] [^n1473]Jesus said unto her, It is not seemly that the children’s bread should be taken and [54] thrown to the dogs. [^n1474]But she said, Yea, my Lord: the dogs also eat of the crumbs [55] that fall from their masters’ tables, and live. [^n1475]Then said Jesus unto her, O woman, [56] great is thy faith: it shall be unto thee as thou hast desired. [^n1476]Go then thy way; and [57] because of this word, the devil is gone out of thy daughter. [^n1477]And her daughter was [58] healed in that hour. [^n1478]And that woman went away to her house, and found her daughter laid upon the bed, and the devil gone out of her.

### Section XXI

Section XXI.

[1] [^n1479]And Jesus went out again from the borders of Tyre and Sidon, and came to the [2] sea of Galilee, towards the borders of Decapolis. [^n1480]And they brought unto him one dumb and deaf, and entreated him that he would lay his hand upon him and heal [3] him. [^n1481]And he drew him away from the multitude, and went away alone, and spat [4] upon his fingers, and thrust them into his ears, and touched his tongue; [^n1482]and looked [5] to heaven, and sighed, and said unto him, Be opened. [^n1483]And in that hour his ears [6] were opened, and the bond of his tongue was loosed, and he spake with ease. [^n1484]And Jesus charged them much that they should not tell this to any man: but the more [7] he charged them, the more they increased in publishing, and marvelled much, [^n1485]and [Arabic, p. 82] said, This man doeth everything well: he made the deaf to hear, and those that lacked speech to speak.

[8, 9] [^n1486]And while he was passing through the land of Samaria, [^n1487]he came to one of the cities of the Samaritans, called Sychar, beside the field which Jacob gave to Joseph to [10] his son. [^n1488]And there was there a spring of water of Jacob’s. And Jesus was fatigued from the exertion of the way, and sat at the spring. And the time was about the [11] sixth hour.[^n1489] [^n1490]And a woman of Samaria came to draw water; and Jesus said unto [12] her, Give me water, that I may drink. [^n1491]And his disciples had entered into the city [13] to buy for themselves food. [^n1492]And that Samaritan woman said unto him, How dost thou, being a Jew, ask me to give thee to drink, while I am a Samaritan woman? [14] [^n1493](And the Jews mingle not with the Samaritans.[^n1494]) Jesus answered and said unto her, If thou knewest the gift of God, and who this is that said unto thee, Give me [15] to drink; thou wouldest ask him, and he would give thee the water of life. [^n1495]That woman said unto him, My Lord, thou hast no bucket, and the well is deep: from [16] whence hast thou the water of life? [^n1496]Can it be that thou art greater than our father Jacob, who gave us this well, and drank from it, and his children, and his sheep? [17] [^n1497]Jesus answered and said unto her, Every one that drinketh of this water shall thirst [18] again: [^n1498]but whosoever drinketh of the water which I shall give him shall not thirst for ever: but the water which I shall give him shall be in him a spring of water springing [19] up unto eternal life. [^n1499]That woman said unto him, My Lord, give me of this water, that [20] I may not thirst again, neither come and draw water from here. [^n1500]Jesus said unto her, [21] [Arabic, p. 83] Go and call thy husband, and come hither. [^n1501]She said unto him, I have no [22] husband. [^n1502]Jesus said unto her, Thou saidst well, I have no husband: five husbands hast thou had, and this man whom thou hast now is not thy husband; and [23] in this thou saidst truly. [^n1503]That woman said unto him, My Lord, I perceive thee to [24] be a prophet. [^n1504]Our fathers worshipped in this mountain; and ye say that in Jerusalem [25] is the place in which worship must be. [^n1505]Jesus said unto her, Woman, believe me, an hour cometh, when neither in this mountain, nor yet in Jerusalem, shall ye worship [26] the Father. [^n1506]Ye worship that which ye know not: but we worship that which [27] we know: for salvation is of the Jews. [^n1507]But an hour cometh, and now is, when the true worshippers shall worship the Father in spirit and truth: and the Father also [28] seeketh such as these worshippers. [^n1508]For God is a Spirit: and they that worship him [29] must worship him in spirit and in truth. [^n1509]That woman said unto him, I know that [30] the Messiah cometh: [^n1510]and when he is come, he will teach us everything. Jesus said unto her, I that speak unto thee am he.

[31] [^n1511]And while he was speaking, his disciples came; and they wondered how he would speak[^n1512] with a woman; but not one of them said unto him, What seekest thou? or, [32] What[^n1513] speakest thou with her? [^n1514]And the woman left her waterpot, and went to the [33] city, and said to the people, [^n1515]Come, and see a man who told me all that ever I did: [34] perhaps then he is the Messiah. [^n1516]And people went out from the city, and came to [35] him. [^n1517]And in the mean while his disciples besought him, and said unto him, Our [36, 37] master, eat. [^n1518]And he said unto them, I have food to eat that ye know not. [^n1519]And the disciples said amongst themselves, Can any one have brought him aught to eat?[^n1520] [38] [^n1521]Jesus said unto them, My food is to do the will of him that sent me, and to accomplish [39] [Arabic, p. 84] his work. [^n1522]Said ye not that after four months cometh the harvest? behold, I therefore say unto you, Lift up your eyes, and behold the lands, [40] that they have become white, and the harvest is already come.[^n1523] [^n1524]And he that reapeth receiveth his wages, and gathereth the fruit of eternal life;[^n1525] and the sower and [41] the reaper rejoice together. [^n1526]For in this is found the word of truth, One soweth, and [42] another reapeth. [^n1527]And I sent you to reap that in which ye have not laboured: others laboured, and ye have entered on their labour.

[43] [^n1528]And from that city many of the Samaritans believed in him because of the words [44] of that woman, who testified and said, He told me all that ever I did. [^n1529]And when those Samaritans came unto him, they besought him to abide with them; and he [45, 46] abode with them two days. [^n1530]And many believed in him because of his word; [^n1531]and they said to that woman, Now not because of thy saying have we believed in him: we have heard and known that this truly is the Messiah, the Saviour of the world.

[47, 48] [^n1532]And after two days Jesus went out thence and departed to Galilee. [^n1533]And Jesus [49] testified that a prophet is not honoured in his own city. [^n1534]And when he came to Galilee, the Galilæans received him.

### Section XXII

Section XXII.

[1] [^n1535]And when Jesus came to a certain village, there drew near to him a leper, and fell at his feet, and besought him, and said unto him, If thou wilt, thou art able to [2] cleanse me. [^n1536]And Jesus had mercy upon him, and stretched forth his hand, and [3] touched him, and said, I will cleanse[^n1537] thee. [^n1538]And immediately his leprosy departed [4] from him, and he was cleansed. [^n1539]And he sternly charged him, and sent him out, [5] [Arabic, p. 85] and said unto him, [^n1540]See that thou tell not any man: but go and shew thyself to the priests, and offer an offering for thy cleansing as Moses commanded [6] for their testimony. [^n1541]But he, when he went out, began to publish much, and spread abroad the news, so that Jesus could not enter into any of the cities openly, for the extent to which the report of him spread, but he remained without in a desert [7] place. [^n1542]And much people came unto him from one place and another,[^n1543] to hear [8] his word, and that they might be healed of their pains. [^n1544]And he used to withdraw from them into the desert, and pray.

[9] [^n1545]And after that, was the feast of the Jews; and Jesus went up to Jerusalem.

[10] [^n1546]And there was in Jerusalem a place prepared for bathing,[^n1547] which was called in [11] Hebrew the House of Mercy, having five porches. [^n1548]And there were laid in them much people of the sick, and blind, and lame, and paralysed, waiting for the moving [12] of the water. [^n1549]And the angel from time to time went down into the place of bathing,[^n1550] and moved the water; and the first that went down after the moving [13] of the water, every pain that he had was healed. [^n1551]And a man was there who had a [14] disease for thirty-eight years. [^n1552]And Jesus saw this man laid, and knew[^n1553] that he had [15] been thus a long time; and he said unto him, Wouldest thou be made whole? [^n1554]That diseased one answered and said, Yea, my Lord, I have no man, when the water moveth, to put me into the bathing-place; but when I come, another goeth down before [16, 17] me. [^n1555]Jesus said unto him, Rise, take thy bed, and walk. [^n1556]And immediately that man was healed; and he rose, and carried his bed, and walked.

[18] [^n1557]And that day was a sabbath. And when the Jews saw that healed one, they[^n1558] said [19] unto him, It is a sabbath: thou hast no authority to carry thy bed. [^n1559]And he answered and said unto them, He that made me whole, the same said unto me, Take thy bed, [20] [Arabic, p. 86] and walk. [^n1560]They asked him therefore, Who is this man that said unto thee, [21] Take thy bed, and walk? [^n1561]But he that was healed knew not who it was; for Jesus had removed from that place to another, because of the press of the great multitude [22] which was in that place. [^n1562]And after two days Jesus happened upon him in the temple, and said unto him, Behold, thou art whole: sin not again, lest there come upon [23] thee what is worse than the first. [^n1563]And that man went, and said to the Jews that it [24] was Jesus that had healed him. [^n1564]And because of that the Jews persecuted Jesus and [25] sought to kill him, because he was doing this on the sabbath. [^n1565]And Jesus said unto [26] them, My Father worketh until now, and I also work. [^n1566]And because of this especially the Jews sought to kill him, not because he profaned the sabbath only; but for his saying also that God was his Father, and his making himself equal with God. [27] [^n1567]Jesus answered and said unto them, Verily, verily, I say unto you, The Son cannot do anything of himself, but what he seeth the Father do; what the Father doeth, [28] that the Son also doeth like him. [^n1568]The Father loveth his Son, and everything that he doeth he sheweth him: and more than these works will he shew him, that ye [29] may marvel. [^n1569]And as the Father raiseth the dead and giveth them life, so the Son [30] also giveth life to whomsoever he will. [^n1570]And the Father judgeth no man, but hath [31] given all judgement unto the Son; [^n1571]that every man may honour the Son, as he honoureth the Father. And he that honoureth not the Son honoureth not the Father which [32] sent him. [^n1572]Verily, verily, I say unto you, Whosoever heareth my word, and believeth in him that sent me, hath eternal life, and cometh not into judgement, but passeth from [33] [Arabic, p. 87] death unto life. [^n1573]Verily, verily, I say unto you, An hour shall come, and now is also, when the dead shall hear the voice of the Son of God; and those [34] which hear shall live. [^n1574]And as the Father hath life in himself,[^n1575] likewise he gave to [35] the Son also that he might have life in himself,[^n1576] [^n1577]and authority to do judgement also, [36] because[^n1578] he is the Son of man. [^n1579]Marvel not then at that: I mean the coming of the hour when all that are in the tombs shall hear his voice, and shall come forth: [37] [^n1580]those that have done good, to the resurrection of life; and those that have done evil deeds, to the resurrection of judgement.

[38] [^n1581]I am not able of myself to do anything; but as I hear, I judge: and my judgement [39] is just; I seek not my own will, but the will of him that sent me. [^n1582]I[^n1583] bear witness [40] of myself, and so[^n1584] my witness is not true. [^n1585]It is another that beareth witness [41] of me; and I know that the witness which he beareth of me is true. [^n1586]Ye have sent [42] unto John, and he hath borne witness of the truth. [^n1587]But not from man do I seek [43] witness; but I say that ye may live.[^n1588] [^n1589]That[^n1590] was a lamp which shineth and [44] giveth light: and ye were pleased to glory now[^n1591] in his light. [^n1592]But I have witness greater than that of John: the works which my Father hath given me to accomplish, [45] those works which I do, bear witness of me, that the Father hath sent me. [^n1593]And the Father which sent me, he hath borne witness of me. Ye have neither heard his [46] voice at any time, nor seen his appearance. [^n1594]And his word abideth not in you; because [47] in him whom he hath sent ye do not believe. [^n1595]Search the scriptures, in which ye rejoice[^n1596] [48] that ye have eternal life; [^n1597]and they bear witness of me; and ye do not wish to come to [49, 50] [Arabic, p. 88] me, that ye may have eternal life. [^n1598]I seek not praise of men. [^n1599]But I know [51] you, that the love of God is not in you. [^n1600]I am come in the name of my Father, and ye received me not; but if another come in his own name, that one will [52] ye receive. [^n1601]And how can ye believe, while ye receive praise one from another, and [53] praise from God, the One, ye seek not? [^n1602]Can it be that ye think that I will accuse you before the Father? Ye have one that accuseth you, Moses, in whom ye have [54] rejoiced.[^n1603] [^n1604]If ye believed Moses, ye would believe me also; Moses wrote of me. [55] [^n1605]And if ye believed not his writings, how shall ye believe my words?

### Section XXIII

Section XXIII.

[1] [^n1606]And Jesus departed thence, and came to the side of the sea of Galilee, and went [2] up into the mountain, and sat there. [^n1607]And there came unto him great multitudes, having with them lame, and blind, and dumb, and maimed, and many others, and [3] they cast them at the feet of Jesus: [^n1608]for they had seen all the signs which he did in [4] Jerusalem, when they were gathered at the feast. [^n1609]And he healed them all. [^n1610]And those multitudes marvelled when they saw dumb men speak, and maimed men healed, and lame men walk, and blind men see; and they praised the God of Israel.

[5] [^n1611]And Jesus called his disciples, and said unto them, I have compassion on this multitude, because of their continuing with me three days, having nothing to eat; and to send them away fasting I am not willing, lest they faint in the way, [^n1612]some of them having [6] [Arabic, p. 89] come from far. [^n1613]His disciples said unto him, Whence have we in the desert [7] bread wherewith to satisfy all this multitude? [^n1614]Jesus said unto them, How [8] many loaves have ye? [^n1615]They said unto him, Seven, and a few small fishes. And he [9] commanded the multitudes to sit down upon the ground; [^n1616]and he took those seven loaves and the fish, and blessed, and brake, and gave to his disciples to set before [10] them; and the disciples set before the multitudes. [^n1617]And they all ate, and were satisfied: and they took that which remained over of the fragments, seven basketfuls.
