[13] [^n1621]And the Pharisees and Sadducees came to him, and began to seek a discussion with him. And they asked him to shew them a sign from heaven, tempting him. [14] [^n1622]And Jesus sighed within himself, and said, What sign seeketh this evil and adulterous generation? It seeketh a sign, and it shall not be given a sign, except the sign [15] of Jonah the prophet. [^n1623]Verily I say unto you, This generation shall not be given a [16] sign. [^n1624]And he left[^n1625] them, and went up into the boat, and went away to that side.

[17] [^n1626]And his disciples forgot to take with them bread, and there was not with them [18] in the boat, not even[^n1627] one loaf. [^n1628]And Jesus charged them, and said, Take heed, and guard yourselves from the leaven of the Pharisees and Sadducees, and from the [19] leaven of Herod. [^n1629]And they reflected within themselves that they had taken with them [20] no bread. [^n1630]And Jesus knew, and said unto them, Why[^n1631] think ye within yourselves, O ye of little faith, and are anxious, because ye have no bread? [^n1632]until now do ye not perceive, [21] neither understand? is your heart yet hard? [^n1633]And have ye eyes, and yet see not? [22] [Arabic, p. 90] and have ye ears, and yet hear not? [^n1634]and do ye not remember when I brake those five loaves for five thousand? and how many baskets full of broken [23] pieces took ye[^n1635] up? They said, Twelve. [^n1636]He said unto them, And the seven also for four thousand: how many baskets full of broken pieces took ye[^n1637] up? They [24] said, Seven. [^n1638]He said unto them, How have ye not understood that I spake not to you because of[^n1639] the bread, but that ye should beware of the leaven of the Pharisees [25] and Sadducees? [^n1640]Then they understood that he spake, not that they should beware of the leaven of the bread, but of the doctrine of the Pharisees and Sadducees, which he called leaven.

[26] [^n1641]And after that, he came to Bethsaida. And they brought to him a certain[^n1642] blind [27] man, and besought him that he would touch him. [^n1643]And he took the hand of that blind man, and led him out without the village, and spat in his eyes, and laid his [28] hand on him,[^n1644] and asked him, What seest thou? [^n1645]And that blind man looked intently, [29] and said unto him, I see men as trees walking. [^n1646]And he placed his hand [30] again on his eyes; and they were restored,[^n1647] and he saw everything clearly. [^n1648]And he sent him to his house, and said, Do not enter even into the village, nor tell any man in the village.

[31] [^n1649]And Jesus went forth, and his disciples, to the villages of Cæsarea Philippi. [32] [^n1650]And while he was going in the way, and his disciples alone,[^n1651] he asked his disciples, [33] and said, What do men say of me that I am, the Son of man?[^n1652] [^n1653]They said unto him, Some say, John the Baptist; and others, Elijah; and others, Jeremiah, or one of the [34, 35] prophets. [^n1654]He said unto them, And ye, what say ye that I am? [^n1655]Simon Cephas answered [36] [Arabic, p. 91] and said, Thou art the Messiah, the Son of the living God. [^n1656]Jesus answered and said unto him, Blessed art thou, Simon son of Jonah: flesh and [37] blood hath not revealed it unto thee, but my Father which is in heaven. [^n1657]And I say unto thee also, that thou art Cephas,[^n1658] and on this rock will I build my church; and the [38] gates of Hades shall not prevail against it. [^n1659]To thee will I give the keys of the kingdom of heaven: and whatsoever thou shalt bind on earth shall be bound in heaven; and [39] whatsoever thou shalt loose on earth shall be loosed in heaven. [^n1660]And he sternly charged his disciples, and warned them that they should not tell any man concerning him, [40] that he was the Messiah. [^n1661]And henceforth began Jesus to shew to his disciples [41] that he was determined[^n1662] to go to Jerusalem, [^n1663]and suffer much, and be rejected of the elders, and of the chief priests, and of the scribes, and be killed, and on the [42] third day rise. [^n1664]And he was speaking[^n1665] plainly. [^n1666]And Simon Cephas, as one grieved [43] for him, said, Far be thou, my Lord, from that. [^n1667]And he turned, and looked upon [44] his disciples, and rebuked Simon, and said, [^n1668]Get thee behind me, Satan: for thou art a stumblingblock unto me: for thou thinkest not of what pertains to God, but of what pertains to men.

[45] [^n1669]And Jesus called the multitudes with his disciples, and said unto them, Whosoever would come after me, let him deny himself, and take his cross every day, and [46] come after me. [^n1670]And whosoever would save his life shall lose it; and whosoever [47] loseth his life for my sake, and for the sake of my gospel, shall save it. [^n1671]What shall [48] a man profit, if he gain all the world, and destroy[^n1672] his own life,[^n1673] or lose it? [^n1674]or what [49] [Arabic, p. 92] will a man give in ransom for his life?[^n1675] [^n1676]Whosoever shall deny me and my sayings in this sinful and adulterous generation, the Son of man also will [50] deny him, when he cometh in the glory of his Father with his holy angels. [^n1677]For the Son of man is about to[^n1678] come in the glory of his Father with his holy angels; and then shall he reward each man according to his works.

### Section XXIV

Section XXIV.

[1] [^n1679]And he said unto them, Verily I say unto you, There be here now some standing that shall not taste death, until they see the kingdom of God come[^n1680] with strength, [^n1681]and the Son of man who cometh in his kingdom.

[2] [^n1682]And after six days Jesus took Simon Cephas, and James, and John his brother, [3] and brought them up into a high mountain, the three of them only. [^n1683]And while they [4] were praying, Jesus changed, and became after the fashion of another person; [^n1684]and his face shone like the sun, and his raiment was very white like the snow, and as [5] the light of lightning, so that nothing on earth can whiten[^n1685] like it. [^n1686]And there appeared [6] unto him Moses and Elijah talking to Jesus. [^n1687]And they thought that the time [7] of his decease which was to be accomplished at Jerusalem was come. [^n1688]And Simon and those that were with him were heavy in the drowsiness of sleep; and with effort they roused themselves, and saw his glory, and those two men that were standing with him. [8] [Arabic, p. 93] [^n1689]And when they began to depart from him, Simon said unto Jesus, My [9] Master, it is good for us to be here: [^n1690]and if thou wilt, we will make here three tabernacles; one for thee, and one for Moses, and one for Elijah; [^n1691]not knowing [10] what he said, because of the fear which took possession of them. [^n1692]And while he [11] was yet saying that, a bright cloud overshadowed them. [^n1693]And when they saw Moses [12] and Elijah that they had entered into that cloud, they feared again. [^n1694]And a voice was heard out of the cloud, saying, This is my beloved Son, whom I have chosen; [13] hear ye therefore him. [^n1695]And when this voice was heard, Jesus was found alone. [14] [^n1696]And the disciples, when they heard the voice, fell on their faces from the fear which [15] took hold of them. [^n1697]And Jesus came and touched them and said, Arise, be not [16] afraid. [^n1698]And they lifted up their eyes, and saw Jesus as he was.

[17] [^n1699]And when they went down from the mountain, Jesus charged them, and said unto them, Tell not what ye have seen to any man, until the Son of man rise from [18] among the dead. [^n1700]And they kept the word within themselves, and told no man in [19] those days what they had seen. [^n1701]And they reflected among themselves, What is this [20] word which he spake unto us, I, when I am risen from among the dead? [^n1702]And his disciples asked him, and said, What is that which the scribes say, then, that Elijah [21] must first come? [^n1703]He said unto them, Elijah cometh first to set in order everything, [Arabic, p. 94] and as it was written of the Son of man, that he should suffer many things, [22] and be rejected. [^n1704]But I say unto you, that Elijah is come, and they knew him not, and have done unto him whatsoever they desired, as it was written of him. [23, 24] [^n1705]In like manner the Son of man is to suffer of them. [^n1706]Then understood the disciples that he spake unto them concerning John the Baptist.

[25] [^n1707]And on that day whereon they came down from the mountain, there met him a multitude of many people standing with his disciples, and the scribes were discussing [26] with them. [^n1708]And the people, when they saw Jesus, were perplexed,[^n1709] and in the [27] midst of their joy hastened[^n1710] and saluted him. [^n1711]And on that day came certain of the Pharisees, and said unto him, Get thee out, and go hence; for Herod seeketh [28] to kill thee. [^n1712]Jesus said unto them, Go ye and say to this fox, Behold, I am casting out demons, and I heal to-day and to-morrow, and on the third day I am perfected. [29] [^n1713]Nevertheless I must be watchful[^n1714] to-day and to-morrow, and on the last day I shall depart; for it cannot be that a prophet perish outside of Jerusalem.

[30] [^n1715]And after that, there came to him a man from that multitude, and fell upon his knees, and said unto him, I beseech thee, my Lord, look upon my son; [^n1716]he is my [31] only child: and the spirit cometh upon him suddenly. [^n1717]A lunacy[^n1718] hath come upon [32] him, and he meeteth with evils. [^n1719]And when it cometh upon him, it beateth him about;[^n1720] [33] and he foameth, and gnasheth his teeth, and wasteth;[^n1721] [^n1722]and many times it hath thrown him into the water and into the fire to destroy him, and it hardly leaveth him after [34] [Arabic, p. 95] bruising him. [^n1723]And I brought him near to thy disciples, and they could [35] not heal him. [^n1724]Jesus answered and said, O faithless and perverse generation, till when shall I be with you? and till when shall I bear with you? bring thy son [36] hither. [^n1725]And he brought him unto him: and when the spirit saw him, immediately [37] it beat him about; and he fell upon the ground, and was raging and foaming. [^n1726]And Jesus asked his father, How long is the time during which he hath been thus? He [38] said unto him, From his youth until now. [^n1727]But, my Lord, help me wherein thou [39] canst, and have mercy upon me. [^n1728]Jesus said unto him, If thou canst believe! All [40] things are possible to him that believeth. [^n1729]And immediately the father of the child [41] cried out, weeping, and said, I believe, my Lord; help my lack of faith. [^n1730]And when Jesus saw the hastening of the people, and their coming at the sound, he rebuked that unclean spirit, and said to it, Thou dumb[^n1731] spirit that speakest not, I command [42] thee,[^n1732] come out of him, and enter not again into him. [^n1733]And that spirit, devil,[^n1734] cried out much, and bruised him, and came out; and that child fell as one dead, and [43] many thought that he had died. [^n1735]But Jesus took him by his hand, and raised him [44] up, and gave him to his father; and that child was healed from that hour. [^n1736]And the people all marvelled at the greatness of God.

[45] [^n1737]And when Jesus entered into the house, his disciples came, and asked him [46] privately,[^n1738] and said unto him, Why were we not able to heal him? [^n1739]Jesus said unto [Arabic, p. 96] them, Because of your unbelief. Verily I say unto you, If ye have faith as a grain of mustard seed, ye shall say to this mountain, Remove hence; [47] and it shall remove; and nothing shall overcome you. [^n1740]But it is impossible to cast out this kind by anything except by fasting and prayer.

[48] [^n1741]And when he went forth thence, they passed through Galilee: and he would not [49] that any man should know it.[^n1742] [^n1743]And he taught his disciples, and said unto them, [50] [^n1744]Keep ye these sayings in your ears and your hearts: for the Son of man is to be delivered into the hands of men, and they shall kill him; and when he is killed, he [51] shall rise on the third day. [^n1745]But they knew not the word which he spake unto them, for it was concealed from them, that they should not perceive it; and they feared to [52] ask him about this word. [^n1746]And they were exceeding sorrowful.

### Section XXV

Section XXV.

[1] [^n1747]And in that day this thought presented itself to his disciples, and they said, which [2] haply should be the greatest among them.[^n1748] [^n1749]And when they came to Capernaum, and entered into the house, Jesus said unto them, What were ye considering in the [3] way among yourselves? [^n1752]And when Cephas entered the house, Jesus anticipated him, and said unto him, What thinkest thou, Simon? the kings of the earth, from whom do they receive custom and tribute? from their sons, or from [6] [Arabic, p. 97] strangers? [14] [^n1765]John answered and said, Our Master, we saw one casting out devils in thy name; [15] and we prevented him, because he followed not thee with us. [18] [^n1770]If thy hand or thy foot injure thee, cut it off, and cast it from thee; for it is better for thee to enter into life being halt or maimed, and not that thou shouldest have two hands or two feet, and fall into the hell of fire that burneth[^n1771] for ever; [19, 20] [^n1772]where their worm dieth not, and their fire is not quenched. [^n1773]And if thine eye seduce[^n1774] [21] thee, pluck it out, and cast it from thee; [^n1775]for it is better for thee to enter the kingdom of God with one eye, than that thou shouldest have two eyes, and fall into the [22, 23] fire of Gehenna; [^n1776]where their worm dieth not, and their fire is not quenched. [^n1779]It is fit neither for the land nor for dung, but they cast it out. He that hath ears to hear, let him [26] hear. [^n1780]Have ye salt in yourselves, and be in peace one with another.

[27] [^n1781]And he arose from thence, and came to the borders of Judæa beyond Jordan: and there went unto him thither great multitudes, and he healed them; and he taught [28] them also, according to his custom. [^n1782]And the Pharisees came unto him, tempting [29] him, and asking him, Is it lawful for a man to put away his wife? [^n1783]He said, What [30] did Moses command you? [^n1785]Jesus answered and said unto them, [^n1786]Have ye not read, He that made them from the beginning [32] made them male and female, and said, [^n1787]For this reason shall the man leave his father [Arabic, p. 99] and his mother, and cleave to his wife; and they both shall be one body? [33] [^n1788]So then they are not twain, but one body; the thing, then, which God hath [34] joined together, let no man put asunder. [^n1789]And those Pharisees said unto him, Why did Moses consent[^n1790] that a man should give a writing of divorcement and put her away? [^n1794]And his disciples, when he entered the house, asked him again [38] about that. [^n1795]And he said unto them, Every one who putteth away his wife, and [39] marrieth another, hath exposed her to adultery. [^n1796]And any woman that leaveth her husband, and becometh another’s, hath committed adultery. [^n1797]And whosoever marrieth [40] her that is divorced hath committed adultery. [^n1798]And his disciples said unto him, If there be between the man and the woman such a case[^n1799] as this, it is not good for [41] a man to marry. [^n1800]He said unto them, Not every man can endure this saying, except [42] him to whom it is given. [^n1801]There are eunuchs which from their mother’s womb[^n1802] were born so; and there are eunuchs which through men became eunuchs; and there are eunuchs which made themselves eunuchs for the sake of the kingdom of heaven. He that is able to be content, let him be content.

[43] [^n1803]Then they brought to him children, that he should lay his hand upon them, and [44] pray: and his disciples were rebuking those that were bringing them. [^n1804]And Jesus saw, and it was distressing to him; and he said unto them, Suffer the children to [Arabic, p. 100] come unto me, and prevent them not; for those that are like these have [45] the kingdom of God. [^n1805]Verily I say unto you, Whosoever receiveth not the [46] kingdom of God as this child, shall not enter it. [^n1806]And he took them in his arms, and laid his hand upon them, and blessed them.

### Section XXVI

Section XXVI.

[1, 2] [^n1807]And there came unto him publicans and sinners to hear his word. [^n1808]And the scribes and the Pharisees murmured, and said, This man receiveth sinners, and [3] eateth with them. [^n1809]And Jesus, when he beheld their murmuring, spake unto them [4] this parable: [^n1810]What man of you, having an hundred sheep, if one of them were lost, would not leave the ninety-nine in the wilderness, and go and seek the straying one [5] till he found it? [^n1811]Verily I say unto you, When he findeth it, he will rejoice over it [6] more than over the ninety-nine that went not astray; [^n1812]and bear it on his shoulders, and bring it to his house, and call his friends and neighbours, [^n1813]and say unto them, [7] Rejoice with me, since I have found my straying sheep. [^n1814]So your Father which is in heaven willeth[^n1815] not that one of these little ones that have strayed should perish, [8] and he seeketh for them repentance. [^n1816]I say unto you, Thus there shall be rejoicing in heaven over one sinner that repenteth, more than over ninety-nine righteous persons that do not need repentance.

[9] [^n1817]And what woman having ten drachmas would lose one of them, and not light a [10] lamp, and sweep the house, and seek it with care till she found it; [^n1818]and when she found it, call her friends and neighbours, and say unto them, Rejoice with me, as I [11] have found my drachma that was lost? [^n1819]I say unto you, Thus there shall be joy [Arabic, p. 101] before the angels of God over the one sinner that repenteth, more than over the ninety-nine righteous persons that do not need repentance.

[12, 13] [^n1820]And Jesus spake unto them also another parable: [^n1821]A man had two sons: and the younger son said unto him, My father, give me my portion that belongeth to [14] me of thy goods. [^n1822]And he divided between them his property. And after a few days the younger son gathered everything that belonged to him, and went into a [15] far country, and there squandered his property by living prodigally. [^n1823]And when he had exhausted everything he had, there occurred a great dearth in that country. [16] [^n1824]And when he was in want, he went and joined himself to one of the people of a city [17] of that country; and that man sent him into the field[^n1825] to feed the swine. [^n1826]And he used to long to fill his belly with the carob that those swine were eating: and no man [18] gave him. [^n1827]And when he returned unto himself, he said, How many hired servants now in my father’s house have bread enough and to spare, while I here perish with [19] hunger! [^n1828]I will arise and go to my father’s house, and say unto him, My father, [^n1829]I [20] have sinned in heaven and before thee, and am not worthy now to be called thy [21] son: make me as one of thy hired servants. [^n1830]And he arose, and came to his father. But his father saw him while he was at a distance, and was moved with compassion [22] for him, and ran,[^n1831] and fell on his breast,[^n1832] and kissed him. [^n1833]And his son said unto him, My father, I have sinned in heaven and before thee, and am not worthy to be [23] called thy son. [^n1834]His father said unto his servants, Bring forth a stately robe, and put [24] it on him; and put a ring on his hand, and put on him shoes on his feet: [^n1835]and bring and [25] slay a fatted ox, that we may eat and make merry: [^n1836]for this my son was dead, and is [26] [Arabic, p. 102] alive; and was lost, and is found. [^n1837]And they began to be merry.[^n1838] Now his elder son was in the field; and when he came and drew near to the house, [27] he heard the sound of many singing.[^n1839] [^n1840]And he called one of the lads, and asked him [28] what this was. [^n1841]He said unto him, Thy brother hath arrived; and thy father hath [29] slain a fatted ox, since he hath received him safe and sound.[^n1842] [^n1843]And he was angry, [30] and would not enter; so his father went out, and besought him to enter. [^n1844]And he said to his father, How many years do I serve thee in bondage, and I never transgressed a commandment of thine; and thou hast never given me a kid, that I might [31] make merry with my friends? [^n1845]but this thy son, when he had squandered thy [32] property with harlots, and come, thou hast slain for him a fatted ox. [^n1846]His father said unto him, My son, thou art at all times with me, and everything I have is [33] thine. [^n1847]It behoveth thee to rejoice and make merry, since this thy brother was dead, and is alive; and was lost, and is found.

[34] [^n1848]And he spake a parable unto his disciples: There was a rich man, and he had [35] a steward; and he was accused to him that he had squandered his property. [^n1849]So his lord called him, and said unto him, What is this that I hear regarding thee? Give me the account of thy stewardship; for it is now impossible that thou shouldest [36] be a steward for me. [^n1850]The steward said within himself, What shall I do, seeing that my lord taketh from me the stewardship? To dig I am not able; and to beg[^n1851] I [37] am ashamed. [^n1852]I know what I will do, that, when I go out of the stewardship, they [38] may receive me into their houses. [^n1853]And he called one after another of his lord’s [39] debtors, and said to the first, How much owest thou my lord? [^n1854]He said unto him, An hundred portions[^n1855] of oil. He said unto him, Take thy writing, and sit down, and write [40] quickly fifty portions.[^n1856] [^n1857]And he said to the next, And thou, how much owest thou my lord? He said unto him, An hundred cors of wheat. He said unto him, Take [41] [Arabic, p. 103] thy writing, and sit down, and write eighty cors. [^n1858]And our[^n1859] lord commended the sinful steward[^n1860] because he had done a wise deed; for the children [42] of this world are wiser than the children of the light in this their age. [^n1861]And I also say unto you, Make unto yourselves friends with the wealth of this unrighteousness;[^n1862] [43] so that, when it is exhausted, they may receive you into their tents for ever. [^n1863]He who is faithful in[^n1864] a little is faithful also in much: and he who is unrighteous in a [44] little is unrighteous also in much. [^n1865]If then in the wealth of unrighteousness ye were [45] not trustworthy, who will intrust you with the truth?[^n1866] [^n1867]If ye are not found faithful in what does not belong to you, who will give you what belongeth to you?

### Section XXVII

Section XXVII.

[1] [^n1868]Therefore the kingdom of heaven is like a certain king, who would make a [2] reckoning with his servants. [^n1869]And when he began to make it, they brought to him one who [3] owed him ten talents.[^n1870] [^n1871]And because he had not wherewith to pay, his lord ordered that he should be sold, he, and his wife, and children, and all that he [4] had, and payment be made. [^n1872]So that servant fell down and worshipped him, and said unto him, My lord, have patience with me, and I shall pay thee everything. [5] [^n1873]And the lord of that servant had compassion, and released him, and forgave him his [6] debt. [^n1874]And that servant went out, and found one of his fellow-servants, who owed him [Arabic, p. 104] a hundred pence;[^n1875] and he took him, and dealt severely with him, and said [7] unto him, Give me what thou owest. [^n1876]So the fellow-servant fell down at his [8] feet, and besought him, and said, Grant me respite, and I will pay thee. [^n1877]And he would not; but took him, and cast him into prison, till he should give him his debt. [9] [^n1878]And when their fellow-servants saw what happened, it distressed them much; and [10] they came and told their lord of all that had taken place. [^n1879]Then his lord called him, and said unto him, Thou wicked servant, all that debt I forgave thee, because [11] thou besoughtest me: [^n1880]was it not then incumbent on thee also to have mercy on thy [12] fellow-servant, as I had mercy on thee?[^n1881] [^n1882]And his lord became wroth, and delivered [13] him to the scourgers, till he should pay all that he owed. [^n1883]So shall my Father which is in heaven do unto you, if one forgive not his brother his wrong conduct[^n1884] from [14] his heart. [^n1885]Take heed within[^n1886] yourselves: if thy brother sin, rebuke him; and if he [15] repent, forgive him. [^n1887]And if he act wrongly towards thee seven times in a day, and on that day return seven times unto thee, and say, I repent towards thee; forgive him. [16] [^n1888]And if thy brother act wrongly towards thee, go and reprove him between thee and [17] him alone: if he hear thee, thou hast gained thy brother. [^n1889]But if he hear thee not, take with thee one or two, and so[^n1890] at the mouth of two or three every saying shall [18] be established. [^n1891]And if he listen not to these also, tell the congregation;[^n1892] and if he listen not even to the congregation, let him be unto thee as a publican and a Gentile.[^n1893] [19] [^n1894]Verily I say unto you, All that ye bind on earth shall be bound in heaven: [20] and what ye loose on earth shall be loosed in heaven. [^n1895]I say unto you also, If two of you agree on earth to ask, everything shall[^n1896] be granted them from my Father [21] [Arabic, p. 105] which is in heaven. [^n1897]For where two or three are gathered in my name, there [22] am I amongst them. [^n1898]Then Cephas drew near to him, and said unto him, My Lord, how many times, if my brother act wrongly towards me, should I forgive him? [23] until seven times? [^n1899]Jesus said unto him, I say not unto thee, Until seven; but, Until seventy [24] times seven, seven.[^n1900] [^n1901]And the servant that knoweth his lord’s will, and maketh not [25] ready for him according to his will, shall meet with much punishment; [^n1902]but he that knoweth not, and doeth something for which he meriteth punishment, shall meet with slight punishment. Every one to whom much hath been given, much shall be asked of him; and he that hath had much committed to him, much shall be [26] required at his hand. [^n1903]I came to cast fire upon the earth; and I would that it had [27] been kindled already.[^n1904] [^n1905]And I have a baptism to be baptized with, and greatly am [28] I straitened till it be accomplished. [^n1906]See that ye despise not[^n1907] one of these little ones that believe in me. Verily I say unto you, Their angels at all times see the [29] face of my Father which is in heaven. [^n1908]The Son of man came to save the thing which was lost.

[30] [^n1909]And after that, Jesus walked in Galilee; and he did not like to walk in Judæa, [31] because the Jews sought to kill him. [^n1910]And there came people who told him of [32] the Galilæans, those whose blood Pilate had mingled with their sacrifices. [^n1911]Jesus answered and said unto them, Do ye imagine that those Galilæans were sinners [33] more than all the Galilæans, so that this thing has come upon them? [^n1912]Nay. Verily I say unto you now,[^n1913] that ye shall all also, if ye repent not, likewise perish. [34] [^n1914]Or perchance those eighteen on whom the palace fell in Siloam, and slew them, do ye imagine that they were to be condemned[^n1915] more than all the people that dwell [35] [Arabic, p. 106] in Jerusalem? Nay. [^n1916]Verily I say unto you, If ye do not all repent, ye shall perish like them.

[36] [^n1917]And he spake unto them this parable: A man had a fig tree planted in his vineyard; [37] and he came and sought fruit thereon, and found none. [^n1918]So he said to the husbandman, Lo, three years do I come and seek fruit on this fig tree, and find [38] none: cut it down; why doth it render the ground unoccupied? [^n1919]The husbandman said unto him, My lord, leave it this year also, that I may dig about it, and dung [39] it; [^n1920]then if it bear fruit—! and if not, then cut it down in the coming year.

[40] [^n1921]And when Jesus was teaching on the sabbath day in one of the synagogues, [41] there was there a woman that had a spirit of disease eighteen years; [^n1922]and she was [42] bowed down, and could not straighten herself at all. [^n1923]And Jesus saw her, and called [43] her, and said unto her, Woman, be loosed from thy disease. [^n1924]And he put his hand [44] upon her; and immediately she was straightened, and praised God. [^n1925]And the chief[^n1926] of the synagogue answered with anger, because Jesus had healed on a sabbath, and said unto the multitudes, There are six days in which work ought to be done; [45] come in them and be healed, and not on the sabbath day. [^n1927]But Jesus answered and said unto him, Ye hypocrites, doth not each of you on the sabbath day loose [46] his ox or his ass from the manger, and go and water it? [^n1928]Ought not this woman, who is a daughter of Abraham, and whom the devil[^n1929] hath bound eighteen years, [47] to be loosed from this bond on the sabbath day? [^n1930]And when he said this, they were all put to shame, those standing, who were opposing him:[^n1931] and all the people were pleased with all the wonders that proceeded from his hand.

### Section XXVIII

Section XXVIII.[^n1932]

[1, 2] [Arabic, p. 107] [^n1933]And at that time the feast of tabernacles of the Jews drew near. [^n1934]So the brethren of Jesus said unto him, Remove now hence, and go to Judæa, that [3] thy disciples may see the deeds that thou doest. [^n1935] For no man doeth a thing secretly [4] and wisheth to be apparent. [^n1936]If thou doest this, shew thyself to the world. For [5] up to this time not even the brethren of Jesus believed on him. [^n1937]Jesus said unto them, My time till now has not arrived; but as for you, your time is alway ready. [6] [^n1938]It is not possible for the world to hate you; but me it hateth, for I bear witness [7] against it, that its deeds are evil. [^n1939]As for you, go ye up unto this feast: but I go [8] not up now to this feast; for my time has not yet been completed. [^n1940]He said this, and remained behind in Galilee.

[9] [^n1941]But when his brethren went up unto the feast, he journeyed from Galilee, and [10] came to the borders of Judæa, to the country beyond Jordan; [^n1942]and there came after [11] him great multitudes, and he healed them all there. [^n1943]And he went out, and proceeded [12] to the feast, not openly, but as one that conceals himself. [^n1944]And the Jews sought him [13] at the feast, and said, In what place is this man? [^n1945]And there occurred much murmuring there in the great multitude that came to the feast, on his account. For [14] some said, He is good: and others said, Nay, but he leadeth the people astray. [^n1946]But no man spake of him openly for fear of the Jews.

[15] [Arabic, p. 108] [^n1947]But when the days of the feast of tabernacles were half over, Jesus went [16] up to the temple, and taught. [^n1948]And the Jews wondered, and said, How doth [17] this man know writing,[^n1949] seeing he hath not learned? [^n1950]Jesus answered and said, My doctrine[^n1951] [18] is not mine, but his that sent me. [^n1952]Whoever wisheth to do his will understandeth my doctrine,[^n1953] whether it be from God, or whether I speak of mine own accord. [19] [^n1954]Whosoever speaketh of his own accord seeketh praise for himself; but whosoever seeketh praise for him that sent him, he is true, and unrighteousness in his heart [20] there is none. [^n1955]Did not Moses give you the law, and no man of you keepeth the [21] law? [^n1956]Why seek ye to kill me? The multitude answered and said unto him, Thou [22] hast demons:[^n1957] who seeketh to kill thee? [^n1958]Jesus answered and said unto them, I did [23] one deed, and ye all marvel because of this. [^n1959]Moses hath given you circumcision (not because it is from Moses, but it is from the fathers); and ye on the sabbath [24] circumcise a man. [^n1960]And if a man is circumcised on the sabbath day, that the law of Moses may not be broken; are ye angry at me, because I healed on the sabbath [25] day the whole man? [^n1961]Judge not with hypocrisy, but judge righteous judgement.

[26] [^n1962]And some people from Jerusalem said, Is not this he whom they seek to slay? [27] [^n1963]And lo, he discourseth with them openly, and they say nothing unto him. Think [28] you that our elders have learned that this is the Messiah indeed? [^n1964]But this man is[^n1965] known whence he is; and the Messiah, when he cometh, no man knoweth whence [29] he is. [^n1966]So Jesus lifted up his voice as he taught in the temple, and said, Ye both know me, and know whence I am; and of my own accord am I not come, but he [30] [Arabic, p. 109] that sent me is true, he whom ye know not: [^n1967]but I know him; for I am [31] from him, and he sent me. [^n1968]And they sought to seize him: and no man [32] laid a hand on him, because his hour had not yet come. [^n1969]But many of the multitude believed on him; and they said, The Messiah, when he cometh, can it be that he will do more than these signs that this man doeth?

[33] [^n1970]And a man of that multitude said unto our Lord, Teacher, say to my brother [34] that he divide with me the inheritance. [^n1971]Jesus said unto him, Man, who is it that [35] appointed me over you as a judge and divider? [^n1972]And he said unto his disciples, Take heed within yourselves of all inordinate desire; for it is not in abundance of [36] possessions that life shall be. [^n1973]And he gave them this parable: The ground of a [37] rich man brought forth abundant produce: [^n1974]and he pondered within himself, and [38] said, What shall I do, since I have no place to store my produce? [^n1975]And he said, I will do this: I will pull down the buildings of my barns, and build them, and make [39] them greater; and store there all my wheat and my goods. [^n1976]And I will say to my soul, Soul, thou hast much goods laid by for many years; take thine ease, eat, [40] drink, enjoy thyself. [^n1977]God said unto him, O thou of little intelligence, this night shall thy soul be taken from thee; and this that thou hast prepared, whose shall it [41] be? [^n1978]So is he that layeth up treasures for himself, and is not rich in God.

[42] [^n1979]And while Jesus was going in the way, there came near to him a young man[^n1980] of the rulers,[^n1981] and fell on his knees, and asked him, and said, Good Teacher, what is [43] it that I must do that I may have eternal life? [^n1982]Jesus said unto him, Why callest thou [44] me good, while there is none good but the one, even God?[^n1983] [^n1984]Thou knowest the commandments.[^n1985] [45] [^n1986]If thou wouldest enter into life, keep the commandments.[^n1987] The young [Arabic, p. 110] man said unto him, Which of the commandments?[^n1988] Jesus said unto him, [46] [^n1989]Thou shalt not commit adultery, Thou shalt not steal, Thou shalt not kill, Thou shalt not bear false witness, Thou shalt not do injury, Honour thy father [47] and thy mother: and, Love thy neighbour as thyself. [^n1990]That young man said unto [48] him, All these have I kept from my youth: what then is it that I lack? [^n1991]And Jesus [49] looked intently at him, and loved him, and said unto him, [^n1992]If thou wouldest be perfect, what thou lackest is one thing:[^n1993] go away and sell everything that thou hast, and give to the poor, and thou shalt have treasure in heaven: and take thy [50] cross, and follow me. [^n1994]And that young man frowned at this word, and went away [51] feeling sad; for he was very rich. [^n1995]And when Jesus saw his sadness, he looked towards his disciples, and said unto them, How hard it is for them that have possessions to enter the kingdom of God!

### Section XXIX

Section XXIX.

[1] [^n1996]Verily I say unto you, It is difficult for a rich man to enter the kingdom of [2] heaven. [^n1998]And the disciples were wondering at these sayings. And Jesus answered and said unto them again, My children, how hard it is for those that rely on their possessions to enter the [4] kingdom of God! [^n1999]And those that were listening wondered more, and said amongst [5] themselves, being agitated,[^n2000] Who, thinkest thou, can be saved? [^n2001]And Jesus looked at them intently, and said unto them, With men this is not possible, but with God it is: [6] [Arabic, p. 111] it is possible for God to do everything. [12] [^n2010]And when the Pharisees heard all this, because of their love for wealth they [13] scoffed at him. [14] [^n2012]And he began to say, A certain man was rich, and wore silk and purple, and enjoyed [15] himself every day in splendour: [^n2013]and there was a poor man named Lazarus, and [16] he was cast down at the door of the rich man, [^n2014]afflicted with sores, and he longed to fill [Arabic, p. 112] his belly with the crumbs that fell from the table of that rich man; yea, [17] even[^n2015] the dogs used to come and lick his sores. [^n2016]And it happened that that poor man died, and the angels conveyed him into the bosom of Abraham: and the [18] rich man also died, and was buried. [^n2017]And while he was being tormented in Hades, [19] he lifted up his eyes from afar, and saw Abraham with[^n2018] Lazarus in his bosom. [^n2019]And he called with a loud voice, and said, My father Abraham, have mercy upon me, and send Lazarus to wet the tip of his finger with water, and moisten my tongue [20] for me; for, behold, I am burned in this flame. [^n2020]Abraham said unto him, My son, remember that thou receivedst thy good things in thy life, and Lazarus his afflictions: [21] but now, behold, he is at rest here, and thou art tormented. [^n2027]He said unto him, Nay,[^n2028] my father Abraham: but [26] let a man from the dead go unto them, and they will repent. [^n2029]Abraham said unto him, If they listen neither to Moses nor to the prophets, neither if a man from the dead rose would they believe him.

[27] [^n2030]The kingdom of heaven is like a man that is a householder, which went out early [28] in the morning to hire labourers for his vineyard. [^n2031]And he agreed with the labourers on [29] one penny a day for each labourer, and he sent them into his vineyard. [^n2032]And he went [30] [Arabic, p. 113] out in three hours, and saw others standing in the market idle. [^n2033]He said unto them, Go ye also into my vineyard, and what is right I will pay you. [31] [^n2034]And they went. And he went out also at the sixth and the ninth hour, and did likewise, [32] and sent them. [^n2035]And about the eleventh hour he went out, and found others standing idle. He said unto them, Why are ye standing the whole day idle? [33] [^n2036]They said unto him, Because no one hath hired us. He said unto them, Go ye [34] also into the vineyard, and what is right ye shall receive. [^n2037]So when evening came, the lord of the vineyard said unto his steward, Call the labourers, and pay them [35] their wages; and begin with the later ones, and end with the former ones. [^n2038]And [36] those of eleven hours[^n2039] came, and received each a penny. [^n2040]When therefore the first came, they supposed that they should receive something more; and they also [37] received each a penny. [^n2041]And when they received it, they spake angrily against the [38] householder, and said, [^n2042]These last worked one hour, and thou hast made them equal [39] with us, who have suffered the heat of the day, and its burden. [^n2043]He answered and said unto one of them, My friend, I do thee no wrong: was it not for a penny that [40] thou didst bargain with me? [^n2044]Take what is thine, and go thy way; for I wish to [41] give this last as I have given thee. [^n2045]Or am I not entitled to do with what is mine[^n2046] [42] what I choose? [43] [^n2048]And when Jesus entered into the house of one of the chiefs of the Pharisees to eat bread on the sabbath day, and they were watching him to see what he would [44, 45] do, [^n2049]and there was before him a man which had the dropsy, [^n2050]Jesus answered and [46] said unto the scribes and the Pharisees, Is it lawful on the sabbath to heal? ### Section XXX

[1] [^n2054]And he spake a parable unto those which were bidden there, because he saw [2] them choose the places that were in the highest part of the sitting room: [^n2055]When a man invites thee to a feast, do not go and sit at the head of the room; lest there [3] be there a man more honourable than thou, [^n2056]and he that invited you come and say unto thee, Give the place to this man: and thou be ashamed when thou risest and [4] takest[^n2057] another place. [^n2058]But when thou art invited, go and sit last; so that when he that invited thee cometh, he may say unto thee, My friend, go up higher: and [5] thou shalt have praise before all that were invited with thee. [^n2059]For every one that exalteth himself shall be abased; and every one that abaseth himself shall be exalted.

[6] [^n2060]And he said also to him that had invited him, When thou makest a feast[^n2061] or a banquet,[^n2062] do not invite thy friends, nor even thy brethren, nor thy kinsmen, nor thy [7] rich neighbours; lest haply they also invite thee, and thou have this reward. [^n2063]But when thou makest a feast, invite the poor, and those with withered hand, and the [8] lame, and the blind: [^n2064]and blessed art thou, since they have not the means to reward [9] thee; that thy reward may be at the rising of the righteous. [^n2065]And when one of them that were invited heard that, he said unto him, Blessed is he that shall eat bread in the kingdom of God.

[10, 11] [^n2066]Jesus answered again in parables, and said, [^n2067]The kingdom of heaven hath been likened [Arabic, p. 115] to[^n2068] a certain king, which made a feast[^n2069] for his son, and prepared a [12] great banquet,[^n2070] and invited many: and he sent his servants at the time of the feast to inform them that were invited, [^n2071]Everything is made ready for you; come. And [13] they would not come, but began all of them with one voice to make excuse. [^n2072]And the first said unto them, Say to him, I have bought a field, and I must needs go out [14] to see it: [^n2073]I pray thee to release[^n2074] me, for I ask to be excused. And another said, I have bought five yoke of oxen, and I am going to examine them: I pray thee [15] to release me, for I ask to be excused. [^n2075]And another said, I have married a wife, [16] and therefore I cannot come. [^n2076]And the king sent also other servants, and said, Say to those that were invited, that my feast is ready, and my oxen and my fatlings are [17] slain, and everything is ready: come to the feast. [^n2077]But they made light of it, and [18] went, one to his field, and another to his merchandise: [^n2078]and the rest took his [19] servants, and entreated them shamefully, and killed them. [^n2079]And one of the servants [20] came, and informed his lord of what had happened. [^n2080]And when the king heard, he became angry, and sent his armies; and they destroyed those murderers, and [21] burned their cities. [^n2081]Then he said to his servants, The feast is prepared, but those [22] that were invited were not worthy. [^n2082]Go out quickly into the markets and into the partings of the ways of the city, and bring in hither the poor, and those with pains, and the lame, and the blind. And the servants did as the king commanded them. [23] [^n2083]And they came, and said unto him, Our lord, we have done all that thou commandedst [24] us, and there is here still room. [^n2084]So the lord said unto his servants, Go out into the roads, and the ways, and the paths, and every one that ye find, invite [25] [Arabic, p. 116] to the feast, and constrain them to enter, till my house is[^n2085] filled. [^n2086]I say unto you, that no one of those people that were invited shall taste of my feast. [^n2088]And the king entered to see those [28] who were seated, and he saw there a man not wearing a festive garment: [^n2089]and he said unto him, My friend, how didst thou come in here not having on festive garments? [1] [^n2183]And when Jesus entered Jerusalem, he went up to the temple of God, and found [2] there oxen and sheep and doves. [^n2199]And Jesus called his disciples, and said unto them, Verily I say unto you, This poor [15] widow cast into the treasury more than all the people: [^n2200]and all of these cast into the place of the offering of God[^n2201] of the superfluity of their wealth; while this woman of her want threw in all that she possessed.

[16] [^n2202]And he spake unto them this parable, concerning people who trusted in themselves [17] that they are righteous, and despised every man: [^n2203]Two men went up to the [18] temple to pray; one of them a Pharisee, and the other a publican. [^n2204]And the Pharisee stood apart,[^n2205] and prayed thus, O Lord, I thank thee, since I am not like the rest of men, the unjust, the profligate, the extortioners, or even like this publican; [19] [^n2206]but I fast two days a week, and tithe all my possessions.[^n2207] [^n2208]And the publican was [20] standing at a distance, and he would not even lift up his eyes to heaven, but was [21] beating upon his breast, and saying, O Lord, have mercy on me, me the sinner. [^n2209]I say unto you, that this man went down justified to his house more than the Pharisee. Every one that exalteth himself shall be abased; and every one that abaseth himself shall be exalted.

[22] [Arabic, p. 123] [^n2210]And when eventide was come, he left all the people, and went outside the [23] city to Bethany, he and his twelve, and he remained there. [^n2211]And all the people, because they knew the place, came to him, and he received them; and them that [24] had need of healing he healed. [^n2212]And on the morning of the next day, when he returned [25] to the city from Bethany, he hungered. And he came unto it, expecting to find something on it; and when he came, he found nothing on it but the leaves—it[^n2215] was not [26] the season of figs—[^n2216]and he said unto it, Henceforward for ever let no man eat fruit of thee. And his disciples heard.

[27] [^n2217]And they came to Jerusalem. And there was there a man of the Pharisees, [28] named Nicodemus, ruler of the Jews. [^n2218]This man came unto Jesus by night, and said unto him, My Master, we know that thou hast been sent from God as a teacher; and no man can do these signs that thou doest, except him whom God is [29] with. [^n2221]Jesus answered and said unto him, Verily, verily, I say unto thee, If a man be not born of water and the Spirit, [32] he cannot enter the kingdom of God. [^n2222]For he that is born of flesh is flesh; and he that [33] is born of Spirit is spirit. [^n2223]Wonder not that I said unto thee that ye must be born a [34] [Arabic, p. 124] second time. [^n2224]The wind bloweth where it listeth, and thou hearest its voice, but thou knowest not from what place it cometh, nor whither it goeth: so [35] is every man that is born of the Spirit. [^n2225]Nicodemus answered and said unto him, [36] How can that be? [^n2226]Jesus answered and said unto him, Art thou teaching[^n2227] Israel, [37] and yet knowest not these things? [^n2228]Verily, verily, I say unto thee, What we know [38] we say, and what we have seen we witness: and ye receive not our witness. [^n2229]If I said unto you what is on earth, and ye believed not, how then, if I say unto you [39] what is in heaven, will ye believe? [^n2230]And no man hath ascended up into heaven, except him that descended from heaven, the Son of man, which is in heaven. [40] [^n2231]And as Moses lifted up the serpent in the wilderness, so is the Son of man to be [41] lifted up; [^n2232]so that every man who may believe in him may not perish, but have [42] eternal life. [^n2233]God so loved the world, that[^n2234] he should give his only Son; and so every one that believeth on him should not perish, but should have eternal life. [43] [^n2235]God sent not his Son into the world to judge the world; but that the world might [44] be saved by his hand. [^n2236]He that believeth in him shall not be judged: but he that believeth not is condemned beforehand, because he hath not believed in the name [45] of the only Son, the Son of God.[^n2237] [^n2238]This is the judgement, that the light came into the world, and men loved the darkness more than the light; because their deeds [46] were evil. [^n2239]Whosoever doeth evil deeds hateth the light, and cometh not to the [47] light, lest his deeds be reproved. [^n2240]But he that doeth the truth cometh to the light, that his deeds may be known, that they have been done in God.

[40] [^n2289]Hear another parable: A man was a householder, and planted a vineyard, and surrounded it with a hedge, and digged in it a winepress, and built in it a tower, [41, 42] [^n2290]and gave it to husbandmen, and went to a distance for a long time. [1] [^n2456]Verily, verily, I say unto you, Whosoever keepeth my word shall not see death [2] for ever. [^n2457]The Jews said unto him, Now we know that thou hast demons. [^n2458]Art thou haply greater than our father Abraham, who is [4] dead, and than the prophets, which are dead? whom makest thou thyself? [^n2459]Jesus said unto them, If I glorify myself, my glory is nothing: my Father is he that [5] glorifieth me; of whom ye say, that he is our[^n2460] God; [^n2461]and yet ye have not known him: but I know him; and if I should say that I know him not, I should become [6] [Arabic, p. 137] a liar like you: but I know him, and keep his word. [^n2462]Abraham your father [7] longed to see my day; and he saw, and rejoiced. [^n2463]The Jews said unto him, [8] Thou art now not fifty years old, and hast thou seen Abraham? [^n2464]Jesus said unto [9] them, Verily, verily, I say unto you, Before Abraham was, I am. [^n2465]And they take[^n2466] stones to stone him: [^n2467]but Jesus concealed himself, and went out of the temple. And he passed through them, and went his way.

[10] [^n2468]And as he passed, he saw a man blind from his mother’s womb. [^n2469]And his [11] disciples asked him, and said, Our Master, who sinned, this man, or his parents, so [12] that he was born blind?[^n2470] [^n2471]Jesus said unto them, Neither did he sin, nor his parents: [13] but that the works of God may be seen in him.[^n2472] [^n2473]It is incumbent on me to do the deeds of him that sent me, while it is day: a night will come, and no man will be [14] able to busy himself. [^n2474]As long as I am in the world, I am the light of the world. [15] [^n2475]And when he said that, he spat upon the ground, and made clay of his spittle, and [16] smeared it on the eyes of the blind man, and said unto him, [^n2476]Go and wash thyself in [17] the pool[^n2477] of Siloam.[^n2478] [^n2479]And he went and washed, and came seeing. And his neighbours, which saw him of old begging, said, Is not this he that was sitting begging? [18] [^n2480]And some said, It is he; and others said, Nay, but he resembles him much. He [19, 20] said, I am he. [^n2481]They said unto him, How then were thine eyes opened? [^n2482]He answered and said unto them, A man named Jesus made clay, and smeared it on my eyes, and said unto me, Go and wash in the water of Siloam: and I went and [21] washed, and received sight.[^n2483] [^n2484]They said unto him, Where is he? He said, I know not. [22, 23] [Arabic, p. 138] [^n2485]And they brought him that was previously blind to the Pharisees. [^n2486]And the day in which Jesus made clay and opened with it his eyes was a sabbath [24] day. [^n2487]And again the Pharisees asked him, How didst thou receive sight? And he said [25] unto them, He put clay on mine eyes, and I washed, and received sight. [^n2488]The people[^n2489] of the Pharisees said, This man is not from God, for he keepeth not the sabbath. And others said, How can a man that is a sinner do these signs? And there came [26] to be a division amongst them. [^n2490]And again they said to that blind man, Thou, then, what sayest thou of him that opened for thee thine eyes? He said unto them, [27] I say that he is a prophet. [^n2491]And the Jews did not believe concerning him, that he was blind, and received sight, until they summoned the parents of him who received [28] sight, and asked them, [^n2492]Is this[^n2493] your son, of whom ye said that he was born blind? [29] how then, behold, doth he now see? [^n2494]His parents answered and said, We know [30] that this is our son, and that he was born blind: [^n2495]but how he has come to see now, or who it is that opened his eyes, we know not: and he also has reached his prime; [31] ask him, and he will speak for himself. [^n2496]This said his parents, because they were fearing the Jews: and the Jews decided, that if any man should confess of him that [32] he was the Messiah, they would put him out of the synagogue. [^n2497]For this reason [33] said his parents, He hath reached his prime; ask him. [^n2498]And they called the man a second time, him that was blind, and said unto him, Praise God: we know that this [34] man is a sinner. [^n2499]He answered and said unto them, Whether he be a sinner, I know [35] not: I know one thing, that I was blind, and I now see. [^n2500]They said unto him again, [36] [Arabic, p. 139] What did he unto thee? how opened he for thee thine eyes? [^n2501]He said unto them, I said unto you, and ye did not hear: what[^n2502] wish ye further to hear? [37] ye also, do ye wish to become disciples to him? [^n2503]And they reviled him, and said unto him, Thou art the disciple[^n2504] of that man; but as for us, we are the disciples of [38] Moses. [^n2505]And we know that God spake unto Moses: but this man, we know not [39] whence he is. [^n2506]The man answered and said unto them, From this is the wonder, [40] because ye know not whence he is, and mine eyes hath he opened. [^n2507]And we know that God heareth not the voice of sinners: but whosoever feareth him, and doeth [41] his will, him he heareth. [^n2508]From eternity hath it not been heard of, that a man [42] opened the eyes of a blind man, who had been born in blindness. [^n2509]If then this man [43] were not from God, he could not do that. [^n2510]They answered and said unto him, Thou wast all of thee born in sins, and dost thou teach us? And they put him forth without.

[44] [^n2511]And Jesus heard of his being put forth without, and found him, and said unto [45] him, Dost thou believe in the Son of God? [^n2512]He that was made whole answered [46] and said, Who is he, my Lord, that I may believe in him? [^n2513]Jesus said unto him, [47] Thou hast seen him, and he that speaketh to thee is he. [^n2514]And he said, I believe, my Lord. And he fell down worshipping him.

### Section XXXVII

Section XXXVII.

[1] [^n2515]And Jesus said, To judge the world am I come, so that they that see not may [2] see, and they that see may become blind. [4] [Arabic, p. 140] [^n2519]Verily, verily, I say unto you, Whosoever entereth not into the fold of the sheep by the door, but goeth up from another place, that man is a thief and a [5, 6] stealer. [^n2521]And therefore[^n2522] the keeper of the door openeth for him the door; and the sheep hear his voice: and [7] he calleth his sheep[^n2523] by their names, and they go forth unto him. [^n2524]And when he putteth forth his sheep, he goeth before them, and his sheep[^n2525] follow him: because [8] they know his voice. [^n2526]And after a stranger will the sheep not go, but they flee from [9] him: because they hear not the voice of a stranger. [^n2527]This parable spake Jesus unto them: but they knew not what he was saying unto them.

[10] [^n2528]Jesus said unto them again, Verily, verily, I say unto you, I am the door of the [11] sheep. [^n2529]And all that came are thieves and stealers: but the sheep heard them not. [^n2531]And the stealer cometh not, save that he may steal, and kill, and destroy: but I came that they might have life, and that they might have [14] the thing that is better.[^n2532] [^n2533]I am the good shepherd; and the good shepherd giveth [15] himself[^n2534] for his sheep. [^n2535]But the hireling, who is not a shepherd, and whose the sheep[^n2536] are not, when he seeth the wolf as it cometh, leaveth the sheep, and fleeth, [16] and the wolf cometh, and snatcheth away the sheep, and scattereth[^n2537] them: [^n2538]and the [17] hireling fleeth because he is an hireling, and hath no care for the sheep. [^n2539]I am the [18] good shepherd; [^n2540]and I know what is mine, and what is mine knoweth me, as my Father knoweth me, and I know my Father; and I give myself[^n2541] for the sheep. [19] [^n2542]And I have other sheep also, that are not of this flock: them also I must invite, and they shall hear my voice; and all the sheep shall be one, and the shepherd one. [20] [Arabic, p. 141] [^n2543]And therefore doth my Father love me, because I give my life, that I may [21] take it again. [^n2544]No man taketh it from me, but I leave it of my own choice. [22] [^n2545]And there occurred a disagreement among the Jews because of these sayings. [25, 26] [^n2549]And the feast of the dedication came on at Jerusalem: and it was winter. [^n2550]And [27] Jesus was walking in the temple in the porch of Solomon. [43] [^n2575]And he went beyond Jordan to the place where John was baptizing formerly; [44] and abode there. [^n2576]And many people came unto him; and they said, John did not [45] work even one sign: but all that John said of this man is truth. [^n2577]And many believed in him.

[^n2582]But Jesus said, This sickness is not unto death, but for the glorifying of God, that the Son of God may be glorified [50, 51] because of it. [^n2588]But if [56] a man walk in the night, he stumbleth, because there is no lamp in him. [31] [^n2630]And the chief priests and the Pharisees gathered, and said, What shall we do? [32] for lo, this man doeth many signs. [1] [Arabic, p. 150] [^n2717]And when Jesus entered the temple, they brought unto him blind and [2] lame: and he healed them.
