[9] [^n2725]Verily, verily, I say unto you, A grain of wheat, if it fall not and die in the [10] earth, remaineth alone; but if it die, it beareth much fruit. [^n2726]He that loveth his life[^n2727] destroyeth it; and he that hateth his life[^n2728] in this world shall keep it unto the life eternal. [11] [^n2729]If a man serve me, he will follow me; and where I am, there shall my servant be [12] also: and whosoever serveth me, the Father will honour him. [^n2730]Now is my soul troubled: [Arabic, p. 151] and what shall I say? My Father, deliver me from this hour. But [13] for this cause came I unto this hour. [^n2731]My Father, glorify thy name. And a [14] voice was heard from heaven, I have glorified it, and shall glorify it. [^n2732]And the multitude that were standing heard, and said, This is thunder: and others said, An [15] angel speaketh to him. [^n2733]Jesus answered and said unto them, Not because of me [16] was this voice, but because of you. [^n2734]Now is the judgement of this world; and the [17] prince of this world shall now be cast forth. [^n2735]And I, when I am lifted up from the [18] earth, shall draw every man unto me. [^n2736]This he said, that he might shew by what [19] manner of death he should die. [^n2737]The multitudes said unto him, We have heard out of the law that the Messiah abideth for ever: how then sayest thou, that the Son of [20] man is to be lifted up? who is this, the Son of man? [^n2738]Jesus said unto them, Another little while is the light with you. Walk so long as ye have light, lest the darkness overtake you; for he that walketh in the darkness knoweth not whither he goeth. [21] [^n2739]So long as ye have light, believe the light, that ye may be the children of the light.

[22] [^n2740]And when certain of the Pharisees asked of Jesus, when the kingdom of God should come, he answered and said unto them, The kingdom of God cometh not [23] with expectation: [^n2741]neither shall they say, Lo, it is here! nor, Lo, it is there! for the kingdom of God is within you.

[24] [^n2742]And in the daytime he was teaching in the temple; and at night he used to go [25] out, and pass the night in the mount called the mount of Olives. [^n2743]And all the people came[^n2744] to him in the morning in the temple, to hear his word.

[26, 27] [^n2745]Then spake Jesus unto the multitudes and his disciples, and said unto them, [^n2746]On [28] [Arabic, p. 152] the seat of Moses are seated the scribes and Pharisees: [^n2747]everything that they say unto you now to keep, keep and do: but according to their deeds [29] do ye not; for they say, and do not. [^n2748]And they bind heavy burdens, and lay them on the shoulders of the people; while they with one of their fingers will not come [30, 31] near[^n2749] them. [^n2750]But all their deeds they do to make a shew before men. [^n2751]And all the multitude were hearing that with pleasure.

[32] [^n2752]And in the course of his teaching he said unto them, Guard yourselves from the [33] scribes, who desire to walk in robes, [^n2753]and love salutation in the marketplaces, and sitting in the highest places of the synagogues, and at feasts in the highest parts of [34] the rooms: [^n2754]and they broaden their amulets, and lengthen the cords of their cloaks, [35] [^n2755]and love that they should be called by men, My master, [^n2756]and devour widows’ houses, because[^n2757] of their prolonging their prayers; these then shall receive greater judgement. [36] [^n2758]But ye, be ye not called masters:[^n2759] for your master is one; all ye are brethren. [37] [^n2760]Call not then to yourselves any one[^n2761] father on earth: for your Father is one, who is [38] in heaven. [^n2762]And be not called directors: for your director is one, even the Messiah. [39, 40] [^n2763]He that is great among you shall be unto you a minister. [^n2764]Whosoever shall exalt himself shall be abased; and whosoever shall abase himself shall be exalted.

[41] [^n2765]Woe unto you, Pharisees! because ye love the highest places in the synagogues, and salutation in the marketplaces.

[42] [^n2766]Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! because ye devour widows’ houses, because[^n2767] of your prolonging your prayers: for this reason then ye shall receive greater judgement.

[43] [^n2768]Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! because ye have shut the kingdom of God before men.

[44] [Arabic, p. 153] [^n2769]Woe unto you that know the law! for ye concealed the keys of knowledge: [^n2770]ye enter not, and those that are entering ye suffer not to enter.

[45] [^n2771]Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! because[^n2772] ye compass land and sea to draw[^n2773] one proselyte; and when he is become so, ye make him a son of hell twice as much[^n2774] as yourselves.

[46] [^n2775]Woe unto you, ye blind guides! because ye say, Whosoever sweareth by the temple, it is nothing; but whosoever sweareth by the gold that is in the temple, [47] shall be condemned.[^n2776] [^n2777]Ye blind foolish ones: which is greater, the gold, or the [48] temple which sanctifieth the gold? [^n2778]And, Whosoever sweareth by the altar, it is nothing; but whosoever sweareth by the offering that is upon it, shall be condemned.[^n2779] [49] [^n2780]Ye blind foolish ones: which is greater, the offering, or the altar which sanctifieth [50] the offering? [^n2781]Whosoever then sweareth by the altar, hath sworn by it, and by all [51] that is upon it. [^n2782]And whosoever sweareth by the temple, hath sworn by it, and by [52] him that is dwelling in it. [^n2783]And whosoever sweareth by heaven, hath sworn by the throne of God, and by him that sitteth upon it.

[53] [^n2784]Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! because ye tithe mint and rue and dill and cummin and all herbs, and ye leave the important matters of the law, judgement, and mercy, and faith, and the love of God: this ought ye to do, and [54] not to leave that undone. [^n2785]Ye blind guides, which strain out a gnat, and swallow[^n2786] camels.

[55] [^n2787]Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! because ye cleanse the outside of the cup and of the platter, while the inside of them is full of injustice and wrong. [56] [^n2788]Ye blind Pharisees, cleanse first the inside of the cup and of the platter, then shall the outside of them be cleansed.

[57] [Arabic, p. 154] [^n2789]Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! because ye resemble whited sepulchres, which appear[^n2790] from the outside beautiful, but within [58] full of the bones of the dead, and all uncleanness. [^n2791]So ye also from without appear unto men like the righteous, but within ye are full of wrong and hypocrisy.

[59] [^n2792]One of the scribes answered and said unto him, Teacher, in this saying of thine [60] thou art casting a slur on us. [^n2793]He said, And to you also, ye scribes, woe! for ye lade men with heavy burdens, and ye with one of your fingers come not near[^n2794] those burdens.

[61] [^n2795]Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! for ye build the tombs of the prophets, which your fathers killed, and adorn the burying-places of the righteous, [62] and say, [^n2796]If we had been in the days of our fathers, we should not have been partakers [63] with them in the blood of the prophets. [^n2797]Wherefore, behold, ye witness against [64] yourselves, that ye are the children of those that slew the prophets. [^n2798]And ye also, [65] ye fill up the measure[^n2799] of your fathers. [^n2800]Ye serpents, ye children of vipers, where shall ye flee from the judgement of Gehenna?

### Section XLI

Section XLI.

[1] [^n2801]Therefore, behold, I, the wisdom of God, am sending unto you prophets, and apostles, and wise men, and scribes: and some of them ye shall slay and crucify; and some of them ye shall beat in your synagogues, and persecute[^n2802] from city to [2] city: [^n2803]that there may come on you all the blood of the righteous that hath been poured upon the ground[^n2804] from the blood of Abel the pure to the blood of Zachariah the son of Barachiah, whom ye slew between the temple[^n2805] and the altar. [3] [^n2806]Verily I say unto you, All these things shall come upon this generation.[^n2807]

[4] [Arabic, p. 155] [^n2808]O Jerusalem, Jerusalem, slayer of the prophets, and stoner of them that are sent unto her! how many times did I wish to gather thy children, as [5] a hen gathereth her chickens under her wings, and ye would not! [^n2809]Your house shall [6] be left over you desolate. [^n2810]Verily I say unto you, Ye shall not see me henceforth, till ye shall say Blessed is he that cometh in the name of the Lord.

[7] [^n2811]And many of the rulers also believed on him; but because of the Pharisees they [8] were not confessing him, lest they be put[^n2812] out of the synagogue: [^n2813]and they loved [9] the praise of men more than the praising of God. [^n2814]And Jesus cried and said, [10] Whosoever believeth in me, believeth not in me, but in him that sent me. [^n2815]And [11] whosoever seeth me hath seen him that sent me. [^n2816]I am come a light[^n2817] into the [12] world, and so every one that believeth in me abideth not in the darkness. [^n2818]And whosoever heareth my sayings, and keepeth them not, I judge him not: for I came [13] not to judge the world, but to give the world life.[^n2819] [^n2820]Whosoever wrongeth[^n2821] me, and receiveth not my sayings, there is one that judgeth him: the word that I spake, it [14] shall judge him at the last day. [^n2822]I from myself did not speak: but the Father which sent me, he hath given me commandment,[^n2823] what I should say, and what I [15] should speak; and I know that his commandment[^n2824] is eternal life. [^n2825]The things that I say now, as my Father hath said unto me, even so I say.

[16] [^n2826]And when he said that unto them, the scribes and Pharisees began their evil-doing, being angry with him, and finding fault with his sayings, and harassing[^n2827] him [17] in many things; [^n2828]seeking to catch something from his mouth, that they might be able to calumniate him.

[18] [^n2829]And when there gathered together myriads of great multitudes, which almost trode [Arabic, p. 156] one upon another, Jesus began to say unto his disciples, Preserve yourselves [19] from the leaven of the Pharisees, which is hypocrisy. [^n2830]For there is nothing [20] concealed, that shall not be revealed: nor hid, that shall not be known. [^n2831]Everything that ye have said in the darkness shall be heard in the light; and what ye have spoken secretly in the ears in the inner chambers shall be proclaimed on the roofs.

[21, 22] [^n2832]This said Jesus, and he went and hid himself from them. [^n2833]But notwithstanding [23] his having done all these signs before them, they believed not in him: [^n2834]that the word of Isaiah the prophet might be fulfilled, who said,

My Lord, who is he that hath believed to hear us?

And the arm of the Lord, to whom hath it appeared?

[24] [^n2835]And for this reason it is not possible for them to believe, because Isaiah also said,

[25] [^n2836]They have blinded their eyes, and made dark their heart;

That they may not see with their eyes, and understand with their heart,

And turn,

So that I should heal them.

[26] [^n2837]This said Isaiah when he saw his glory, and spake of him.

[27] [^n2838]And when Jesus went out of the temple, certain of his disciples came forward [28] to shew[^n2839] him the buildings of the temple, [^n2840]and its beauty and greatness, and the strength of the stones that were laid in it, and the elegance of its building, and that [29] it was adorned with noble stones and beautiful colours. [^n2841]Jesus answered and said [30] unto them, See ye these great buildings? [^n2842]verily I say unto you, Days will come, when there shall not be left here a stone upon another, that shall not be cast down.

[31] [^n2843]And two days before[^n2844] the passover of unleavened bread, the chief priests and [32] the scribes sought how they might take him by deceit,[^n2845] and kill him: [^n2846]and they said, It shall not be at the feast, lest the people be agitated.

[33] [^n2847]And when Jesus sat on the mount of Olives opposite the temple, his disciples, Simon Cephas and James and John and Andrew, came forward unto him, and said unto him [34] between themselves and him, [^n2848]Teacher, tell us when that shall be, and what is the sign [35] [Arabic, p. 157] of thy coming and the end of the world. [^n2849]Jesus answered and said unto them, Days will come, when ye shall long to see one of the days of the Son of [36, 37] [^n2850]man, and shall not behold. [^n2851]Take heed lest any man lead you astray. Many shall [38] come in my name, and say, I am the Messiah; [^n2852]and they shall say, The time is come [39] near, and shall lead many astray: go not therefore after them. [^n2853]And when ye hear of wars and tidings of insurrections, see to it, be[^n2854] not agitated: for these things must [40] first be; only the end is not yet come. [^n2855]Nation shall rise against nation, and kingdom [41] against kingdom: [^n2856]and great earthquakes shall be in one place and another, and there shall be famines and deaths and agitations: and there shall be fear and terror and great signs that[^n2857] shall appear from heaven, and there shall be great [42, 43] storms [^n2858]All these things are the beginning of travail. [^n2859]But before all of that, they shall lay hands upon you, and persecute you, and deliver you unto the synagogues [44] and into prisons, and bring you before kings and judges for my name’s sake. [^n2860]And [45] that shall be unto you for a witness. [^n2861]But first must my gospel be preached unto all [46] nations. [^n2862]And when they bring you into the synagogues before the rulers and the authorities, be not anxious beforehand how ye shall answer for yourselves, or what ye [47, 48] shall say: [^n2863]because it is not ye that speak, but the Holy Spirit. [^n2864]Lay it to your heart, not [49] [Arabic, p. 158] to be anxious before the time what ye shall say: [^n2865]and I shall[^n2866] give you understanding and wisdom,[^n2867] which all your adversaries shall not be able to gainsay. [50] [^n2868]And then shall they deliver you unto constraint, and shall kill you: and ye shall be [51] hated of all nations because of my name. [^n2869]And then shall many go astray,[^n2870] and they [52] shall hate one another, and deliver one another unto death. [^n2871]And your parents, and your brethren, and your kinsfolk, and your friends shall deliver you up, and shall [53, 54] slay some of you. [^n2872]But a lock of hair from your heads shall not perish. [^n2873]And by [55] your patience ye shall gain[^n2874] your souls. [^n2875]And many men,[^n2876] false prophets, shall arise, [56] and lead many astray. [^n2877]And because of the abounding of iniquity, the love of many [57] shall wax cold. [^n2878]But he that endureth to the end, the same shall be saved. [^n2879]And [58] this, the[^n2880] gospel of the kingdom, shall be preached in all the world for a testimony to all nations; and then shall come the end of all.

### Section XLII

Section XLII.

[1] [^n2881]But when ye see Jerusalem with the army compassing it about, then know that [2] its desolation is come near. [^n2882]Those then that are in Judæa at that time shall flee to the mountain; and those that are within her shall flee; and those that are in the [3] villages shall not enter her. [^n2883]For these days are the days of vengeance, that all that [4] is written may be fulfilled. [^n2884]And when ye see the unclean sign of desolation,[^n2885] spoken of in Daniel the prophet, standing in the pure place, he that readeth shall understand, [5, 6] [^n2886]and then he that is in Judæa shall flee in to the mountain: [^n2887]and let him that is on the [7] roof not go down, nor enter in to take anything from his house: [^n2888]and let him that is in [8] [Arabic, p. 159] the field not turn behind him to take his garment. [^n2889]Woe to them that are with child and to them that give suck in those days! there shall be great [9] distress in the land, and wrath against this nation. [^n2890]And they shall fall on the edge of the sword,[^n2891] and shall be taken captive to every land: and Jerusalem shall be trodden down of the nations, until the times of the nations be ended.

[10] [^n2892]Then if any man say unto you, The Messiah is here; or, Lo, he is there; believe [11] him not: [^n2893]there shall rise then false Messiahs and prophets of lying, and shall do signs and wonders, in order that they may lead astray even the elect also, if they [12] be able. [^n2894]But as for you, beware: for I have acquainted you with everything [13] beforehand. [^n2895]If then they say unto you, Lo, he is in the desert; go not out, lest ye [14] be taken: and if they say unto you, Lo, he is in the chamber; believe not. [^n2896]And as the lightning appeareth from the east, and is seen unto the west; so shall be the [15] coming of the Son of man. [^n2897]But first he must suffer much and be rejected by this [16] generation.[^n2898] [^n2899]Pray therefore that your flight be not in winter, nor on a sabbath: [17] [^n2900]there shall be then great tribulation,[^n2901] the like of which there hath not been from the [18] beginning of the world till now, nor shall be. [^n2902]And except the Lord had shortened those days, no flesh would have lived: but because of the elect, whom he elected, [19] he shortened those days. [^n2903]And there shall be signs in the sun and the moon and the stars; and upon the earth affliction[^n2904] of the nations, and rubbing of hands for the confusion[^n2905] [20] [Arabic, p. 160] of the noise of the sea, and an earthquake: [^n2906]the souls of men shall [21] go forth from fear of that which is to come upon the earth. [^n2907]And in those days, straightway after the distress of those days, the sun shall become dark, and the moon shall not shew its light, and the stars shall fall from heaven, and the powers [22] of heaven shall be convulsed: [^n2908]and then shall appear the sign of the Son of man in heaven: and at that time all the tribes of the earth shall wail, and look unto the Son [23] of man coming on the clouds of heaven with power and much glory. [^n2909]And he shall send his angels with the great trumpet, and they shall gather his elect from the four [24] winds, from one end of heaven to the other.[^n2910] [^n2911]But when these things begin to be, be of good cheer, and lift up your heads; for your salvation[^n2912] is come near.

[25] [^n2913]Learn the example of the fig tree: when it letteth down its branches,[^n2914] and putteth [26] forth its leaves, ye know that the summer is come; [^n2915]so ye also, when ye see these things begun to be, know ye that the kingdom of God hath arrived at the [27] door. [^n2916]Verily I say unto you, This generation shall not pass away, until all these [28] things shall be. [^n2917]Heaven and earth shall pass away, but my sayings shall not pass away.

[29] [^n2918]Take heed to yourselves, that your hearts become not heavy with inordinate desire,[^n2919] and drunkenness, and the care of the world at any time, and that day come [30] upon you suddenly: [^n2920]for it is as a shock that shocks all the inhabitants that are on the [31] face of the whole earth. [^n2921]Watch at all times, and pray, that ye may be worthy to escape [Arabic, p. 161] from all the things that are to be, and that ye may stand before the Son of [32] man. [^n2922]Of that day and of that hour hath no man learned, not even the angels [33] of heaven, neither the Son, but the Father. [^n2923]See ye, and watch and pray: for ye know [34] not when that time will be. [^n2924]It is as a man, who journeyed, and left his house, and gave his authority to his servants, and appointed every man to his work, and [35] charged the porter to be wakeful. [^n2925]Be wakeful then:[^n2926] since ye know not when the lord of the house cometh, in the evening, or in the middle of the night, or when the [36] cock croweth, or in the morning; [^n2927]lest he come unexpectedly, and find you sleeping. [37] [^n2928]The thing that I say unto you, unto all of you do I say it, Be ye watchful.

[38] [^n2929]For as it was in the days of Noah, so shall the coming of the Son of man be. [39] [^n2930]As they were before the flood eating and drinking, and taking wives, and giving [40] wives to men, [^n2931]until the day in which Noah entered into the ark, and they perceived not till the flood came, and took them all; so shall the coming of the Son of man [41] be. [^n2932]And as it was in the days of Lot; they were eating and drinking, and selling [42] and buying, and planting and building, [^n2933]on the day in which Lot went out from Sodom, and the Lord rained fire and brimstone from heaven, and destroyed them [43, 44] all: [^n2934]so shall it be in the day in which the Son of man is revealed.[^n2935] [^n2936]And in that day, whosoever is on the roof, and his garments[^n2937] in the house, let him not go down to [45] take them: and he that is in the field shall not turn behind him. [^n2938]Remember Lot’s [46] wife. [^n2939]Whosoever shall desire to save his life shall destroy it: but whosoever shall [47] destroy his life shall save it. [^n2940]Verily I say unto you, In that night there shall be two on [48] [Arabic, p. 162] one bed; one shall be taken, and another left. [^n2941]And two women shall be grinding [49] at one mill; one shall be taken, and another left. [^n2942]And two shall be in the [50] field; one shall be taken, and another left. [^n2943]They answered and said unto him, To what place, our Lord? He said unto them, Where the body is, there will the eagles [51, 52] gather. [^n2944]Be attentive now: for ye know not at what hour your Lord cometh. [^n2945]Know this: if the master of the house had known in what watch the thief would come, he would have been attentive, and would not make it possible that his house should be [53] broken through. [^n2946]Therefore be ye also ready: for in the hour that ye think not the Son of man cometh.

### Section XLIII

Section XLIII.

[1] [^n2947]Simon Cephas said unto him, Our Lord, is it to us that thou hast spoken this [2] parable, or also to every man? [^n2948]Jesus said unto him, Who, thinkest thou, is the servant, the master of the house,[^n2949] trusted with control,[^n2950] whom his lord set over his [3] household, to give them their food in its season? [^n2951]Blessed is that servant, whom his [4] lord shall come and find having done so. [^n2952]Verily I say unto you, He will set him [5] over all that he hath. [^n2953]But if that evil servant say in his heart, My lord delayeth his [6] coming; [^n2954]and shall begin to beat his servants and the maidservants of his lord, and [7] shall begin to eat and to drink with the drunken; [^n2955]the lord of that servant shall come [8] in the day that he thinketh not, and in the hour that he knoweth not, [^n2956]and shall [Arabic, p. 163] judge him, and appoint his portion with the hypocrites, [^n2957]and with those that are not faithful: [^n2958]there shall be weeping and gnashing of teeth.

[9] [^n2959]Then shall the kingdom of heaven be like unto ten virgins, those that took their [10] lamps, and went forth to meet the bridegroom and the bride. [^n2960]Five of them were [11] wise, and five foolish. [^n2961]And those foolish ones took their lamps, and took not with [12, 13] them oil: [^n2962]but those wise ones took oil in vessels along with their lamps. [^n2963]When then [14] the bridegroom delayed, they all slumbered and slept. [^n2964]But in the middle of the night there occurred a cry, Behold, the bridegroom cometh! Go forth therefore to [15, 16] meet him. [^n2965]Then all those virgins arose, and made ready their lamps. [^n2966]The foolish [17] said unto the wise, Give us of your oil; for our lamps are gone out. [^n2967]But those wise answered and said, Perhaps[^n2968] there will not be enough for us and you: but go ye to [18] the sellers, and buy for yourselves. [^n2969]And when they went away to buy, the bridegroom came; and those that were ready went in with him to the marriage feast: and [19] the door was shut. [^n2970]And at last those other virgins also came and said, Our Lord, [20] our Lord, open unto us. [^n2971]He answered and said unto them, Verily I say unto you, [21] I know you not. [^n2972]Watch then, for ye know not that day nor that hour.

[22] [^n2973]It is as a man, who went on a journey, and called his servants, and delivered unto [23] them his possessions. [^n2974]And unto one he gave five talents,[^n2975] and another two, and another [24] one; every one according to his strength; and went on his journey forthwith. [^n2976]He [Arabic, p. 164] then that received the five talents went and traded with them, and gained [26] other five. [^n2977]And so also he of the two gained other two. [^n2978]But he that received [27] the one went and digged in the earth, and hid the money of his lord. [^n2979]And after a long time the lord of those servants came, and took from them the account. [28] [^n2980]And he that received five talents came near and brought other five, and said, My lord, thou gavest me five talents: lo, I have gained other five in addition to them. [29] [^n2981]His lord said unto him, Well done, thou good and faithful servant: over a little hast [30] thou been faithful, over much will I set thee: enter into the joy of thy lord. [^n2982]And he that had the two came near and said, My lord, thou gavest me two talents: lo, [31] other two have I gained in addition to them. [^n2983]His lord said unto him, Good,[^n2984] thou faithful servant: over a little hast thou been faithful, over much will I set thee: enter [32] into the joy of thy lord. [^n2985]And he also that received the one talent came forward and said, My lord, I knew thee that thou art a severe man, who reapest where thou [33] sowest not, and gatherest where thou didst not scatter: [^n2986]and so I was afraid, and [34] went away and hid thy talent in the earth: lo, thou hast what is thine. [^n2987]His lord answered and said unto him, Thou wicked and slothful servant, thou knewest me [35] that I reap where I sowed not, and gather where I did not scatter; [^n2988]it was incumbent on thee to put my money to the bank,[^n2989] and then I should come and seek it with its [36] gains. [^n2990]Take now from him the talent, and give it to him that hath ten talents. [37] [^n2991]Whosoever hath shall be given, and he shall have more: but he that hath not, even [38] [Arabic, p. 165] what he hath shall be taken from him. [^n2992]And the unprofitable servant, put him forth into the outer darkness: there shall be the weeping and gnashing of teeth.

[39, 40] [^n2993]Your loins shall be girded, and your lamps lit; [^n2994]and ye shall be like the people that are looking for their lord, when he shall return from the feast; so that, when [41] he cometh and knocketh, they may at once open unto him. [^n2995]Blessed are those servants, whom their lord shall come and find attentive: verily I say unto you, that he will gird his waist, and make them sit down, and pass through[^n2996] them and serve [42] them. [^n2997]And if he come in the second watch, or the third, and find thus, blessed are those servants.

[^n3002]Then shall the King say to those that are at his right, Come, ye blessed of my Father, inherit the kingdom prepared for you from the foundations[^n3003] of the world: [47] [^n3004]I hungered, and ye gave me to eat; and I thirsted, and ye gave me to drink; and I [48] was a stranger, and ye took me in; [^n3005]and I was naked, and ye clothed me; and I [49] was sick, and ye visited me; and I was in prison, and ye cared for me. [^n3006]Then shall those righteous say unto him, Our Lord, when saw we thee hungry, and fed thee? [50] or thirsty, and gave thee to drink? [^n3011]Then shall he say unto those that are on his left also, Depart from me, ye cursed, [54] into the eternal fire prepared for the devil and his hosts: [^n3012]I hungered, and ye fed me [55] not; and I thirsted, and ye did not give me to drink; [^n3013]and I was a stranger, and ye took me not in; and I was naked, and ye clothed me not; and I was sick, and imprisoned, [56] and ye visited me not. ### Section XLVI

[1] [^n3165]Whosoever hath my commandments, and keepeth them, he it is that loveth me: and he that loveth me shall be loved of my Father, and I will love him, and will [2] shew myself unto him. [5, 6] [^n3170]This have I spoken unto you, while I was yet with you. [^n3171]But the Paraclete, the Holy Spirit, whom my Father will send in my name, he will teach you everything, and [7] [Arabic, p. 174] he will bring to your remembrance all that I say unto you. [^n3181]He said unto them, Henceforth, whosoever hath a purse, let him take it, and likewise the wallet also: and whosoever hath not a sword, shall sell his garment, and buy for himself a [14] sword. [17] [^n3186]And he said unto them, I am the true vine, and my Father is the husbandman. [18] [^n3187]Every branch that produceth not fruit in me, he taketh it: and that which giveth fruit, [19] he cleanseth it, that it may give much fruit. And as the branch of the [Arabic, p. 175] vine cannot produce fruit of itself, if it be not abiding in the vine; so too ye [21] also, if ye abide not in me. [^n3190]I am the vine, and ye are the branches: He then that abideth in me, and I in him, he giveth much fruit: for without me ye cannot [22] do anything. [^n3191]And if a man abide not in me, he is cast without, like a withered [23] branch; and it is gathered, and cast[^n3192] into the fire, that it may be burned. [^n3193]If ye abide in me, and my word abide in you, everything that ye desire to ask shall be [24] done unto you. [^n3194]And herein is the Father glorified, that ye may give much fruit; [25] and ye shall be my disciples. [^n3195]And as my Father loved me, I loved you also: [26] abide in my love. [^n3196]If ye keep my commands, ye shall abide in my love; as I have [27] kept my Father’s commands, and abode in his love. [^n3197]I have spoken that unto you, [28] that my joy[^n3198] may be in you, and your joy[^n3199] be fulfilled. [^n3200]This is my commandment, [29] that ye love one another, as I loved you. [^n3201]And no love is greater than this, namely, [30] that a man should give his life for his friends. [^n3202]Ye are my friends, if ye do all that [31] I command you. [^n3203]I call you not now servants; for the servant knoweth not what his lord doeth: my friends have I now called you; for everything that I heard from [32] my Father I have made known unto you. [^n3204]Ye did not choose[^n3205] me, but I chose I you, and appointed you, that ye also should go and bear fruit, and that your fruit should[^n3206] [33] abide; and that all that ye shall ask my Father in my name, he may[^n3207] give you. [^n3212]Remember the word that I said unto you, that no servant is greater than his lord. And if they persecuted[^n3213] me, you also will they [37] persecute;[^n3214] and if they kept my word, your word also will they keep. [^n3215]But all these things will they do unto you for my name’s sake, for they have not known[^n3216] him [38] that sent me. [^n3217]And if I had not come and spoken unto them, they had not had sin: [39] but now they have no excuse for their sins. [^n3218]Whosoever hateth me, also hateth my [40] Father. ### Section XLVII

Section XLVII.

[1] [^n3248]A little while, and ye shall not behold me; and a little while again, and ye shall [2] behold me; because I go to the Father. [^n3249]His disciples therefore said one to another, What is this that he hath said unto us, A little while, and ye shall not behold me; and a little while again, and ye shall behold me: and, I go to my [3] Father? [6] [^n3255]For, a woman when the time is come for her that she should bring forth, the arrival of the day of her bringing forth distresseth her: but whenever she hath brought forth a son, she remembereth not her distress, for joy at the birth of a man into the [7] world. [^n3256]And ye now also grieve: but I shall see you, and your hearts shall rejoice, [8] [Arabic, p. 178] and your joy no man taketh from you. [^n3257]And in that day ye shall ask me nothing. [10] [^n3260]I have spoken unto you now in ænigmas:[^n3261] but there will come an hour when[^n3262] I shall not speak to you in ænigmas,[^n3263] but shall reveal unto you the Father plainly, [11] in that day when[^n3264] ye shall ask in my name: [^n3265]and I say not unto you, that I shall [12] entreat the Father for you; [^n3266]for the Father loveth you, because ye have loved me, [13] and have believed that I came forth from my Father. [^n3269]Now, lo, we know that thou knowest everything, and needest not that any man should ask thee: and by this we believe that thou camest forth [16, 17] from God. [^n3272]This have I said unto you, that in me ye may have peace. And in the world trouble shall overtake you: but be of good courage; for I have overcome the world.

[19] [^n3273]This said Jesus, and lifted up his eyes unto heaven, and said, My Father, the hour [20] is come; glorify thy Son, that thy Son may glorify thee: [^n3274]as thou gavest him authority [21] over all flesh, that all that thou hast given him, he might give them[^n3275] eternal life. [^n3276]And this is eternal life, that they should[^n3277] know that thou alone art true God, and that he [22] [Arabic, p. 179] whom thou didst send is Jesus the Messiah.[^n3278] [^n3279]I glorified thee in the earth, [23] and the work which thou gavest me to do I have accomplished. [^n3280]And now glorify thou me, O Father, beside thee, with that glory which I had with thee [24] before the world was. [^n3281]I made known thy name to the men whom thou gavest me out of the world: thine they were, and thou gavest them to me; and they have kept [25, 26] thy word. [^n3282]Now they[^n3283] know that all that thou hast given me is from thee: [^n3284]and the sayings which thou gavest me I have given unto them; and they received them, and knew of a truth that I came forth from thee, and believed that thou didst send me. [27] [^n3285]And I ask for their sake: and my asking is not for the world, but for those whom [28] thou hast given me; for they are thine: [^n3286]and all that is mine is thine, and all that is [29] thine is mine: and I am glorified in them. [^n3287]And now I am not in the world, and they are in the world, and I come to thee. My[^n3288] holy Father, keep them in thy [30] name which thou hast given unto me, that they may be one, as we are. [^n3289]When I was with them in the world, I kept them in thy name: and I kept those whom thou gavest unto me: and no man of them hath perished, but the son of perdition; that [31] the scripture might be fulfilled. [^n3290]Now I come to thee: and this I say in the world, [32] that my joy may be complete in them. [^n3291]I have given them thy word; and the world [33] hated them, because they were not of the world, as I was not of the world. [^n3292]And I ask not this, that thou take them from the world, but that thou keep them from the [34, 35] evil one. [^n3293]They were not of the world, as I was not of the world. [^n3294]O Father, sanctify [36] them in thy truth: for thy word is truth. [^n3295]And as thou didst send me into the world, I [37] [Arabic, p. 180] also send them into the world. [^n3296]And for their sake I sanctify myself, that they [38] also may be sanctified in the truth. [^n3297]Neither for these alone do I ask, but for [39] the sake of them that believe in me through their word; [^n3298]that they may be all one; as thou art in me, and I in thee, and so they also shall be one in us: that the world [40] may believe that thou didst send me. [^n3299]And the glory which thou hast given unto [41] me I have given unto them; [^n3300]that they may be one, as we are one; I in them, and thou in me, that they may be perfect into[^n3301] one; and that the world may know that [42] thou didst send me, and that I[^n3302] loved them, as thou lovedst me. [^n3303]Father, and those whom thou hast given me, I wish that, where I am, they may be with me also; that they may behold my glory, which thou hast given me: for thou lovedst me before [43] the foundation[^n3304] of the world. [^n3305]My righteous Father,[^n3306] and the world knew thee not, [44] but I know thee; and they knew that thou didst send me; and I made known unto them thy name, and will make it known to them; [^n3307]that the love wherewith thou lovedst me may be in them, and I shall[^n3308] be in them.

[1] [^n3309]This said Jesus, and went forth with his disciples to a place which was called Gethsemane,[^n3310] on[^n3311] the side that is in the plain[^n3312] of Kidron, the mountain,[^n3313] the place [2] in which was a garden; and he entered thither, he and his disciples. [1] [^n3461]And Pilate said unto the chief priests and the multitude, I have not found [2] against this man anything. [^n3562]Days are coming, when they shall say, Blessed are the barren, and the wombs that bare not, and the breasts [22] that gave not suck. [^n3563]Then shall they begin to say to the mountains, Fall on us; and [23] to the hills, Cover us. [^n3564]For if they do so in the green tree,[^n3565] what shall be in the dry?

[24] [^n3566]And they brought with Jesus two others of the malefactors,[^n3567] to be put to death.

[25] [^n3568]And when they came unto a certain place called The skull, and called in the Hebrew Golgotha, they crucified him there: [^n3569]they crucified with him these two [26] malefactors, one on his right, and the other on his left. [^n3570]And the scripture was [27] [Arabic, p. 194] fulfilled, which saith, He was numbered with the transgressors. [^n3571]And they gave him to drink wine and myrrh, and vinegar which had been mixed with the myrrh; [^n3572]and he tasted, and would not drink; and he received it not.

[28] [^n3573]And the soldiers, when they had crucified Jesus, took his garments, and cast lots for them in four parts, to every party of the soldiers a part; and his tunic was [29] without sewing, from the top woven throughout. [^n3574]And they said one to another, Let us not rend it, but cast lots for it, whose it shall be: and the scripture was fulfilled, which saith,

They divided my garments among them,

And cast the lot for my vesture.

[30, 31] [^n3575]This the soldiers did. And they sat and guarded him there. [^n3576]And Pilate wrote on a tablet the cause of his death, and put it on the wood of the cross above his head.[^n3577] And there was written upon it thus: This is Jesus the Nazarene, the King of the [32] Jews. [^n3578]And this tablet[^n3579] read many of the Jews: for the place where Jesus was crucified was near the city: and it was written in Hebrew and Greek and Latin. [33] [^n3580]And the chief priests said unto Pilate, Write not, The King of the Jews; but, He it is [34] that[^n3581] said, I am the King of the Jews. [^n3582]Pilate said unto them, What hath been [35] written hath been written.[^n3583] [^n3584]And the people were standing beholding; and they [36] that passed by were reviling[^n3585] him, and shaking[^n3586] their heads, and saying, [^n3587]Thou that destroyest the temple, and buildest it in three days, [^n3588]save thyself if thou art the Son [37] of God, and come down from the cross. [^n3589]And in like manner the chief priests and the [Arabic, p. 195] scribes and the elders and the Pharisees derided him, and laughed one with [38, 39] another, and said, [^n3590]The saviour of others cannot save himself. [^n3591]If he is the Messiah, the chosen of God, and the King of Israel,[^n3592] let him come down now from the [40] cross, that we may see, and believe in him. [^n3593]He that relieth on God—let him deliver him [41] now, if he is pleased with him: for he said, I am the Son of God. [^n3594]And the soldiers [42] also scoffed at him in that they came near unto him, [^n3595]and brought him vinegar, and [43] said unto him, If thou art the King of the Jews, save thyself. [^n3596]And likewise the two robbers[^n3597] also that were crucified with him reproached him.

[44] [^n3598]And one of those two malefactors who were crucified with him reviled him, and [45] said, If thou art the Messiah, save thyself, and save us also. [^n3599]But his comrade rebuked him, and said, Dost thou not even fear God, being thyself also in this [46] condemnation? [^n3600]And we with justice, and as we deserved, and according to our deed,[^n3601] have we been rewarded: but this man hath not done anything unlawful. [47] [^n3602]And he said unto Jesus, Remember me, my Lord, when thou comest in thy kingdom. [48] [^n3603]Jesus said unto him, Verily[^n3604] I say unto thee, To-day shalt thou be with me in Paradise.

[49] [^n3605]And there stood by the cross of Jesus his mother, and his mother’s sister,[^n3606] [50] Mary[^n3607] that was related to Clopas, and Mary Magdalene. [^n3608]And Jesus saw his mother, and that disciple whom he loved standing by; and he said to his mother, [51] Woman, behold, thy son! [^n3609]And he said to that disciple, Behold, thy mother! And from that hour that disciple took her unto himself.

[52] [Arabic, p. 196] [^n3610]And from the sixth hour[^n3611] darkness was on all the land unto the ninth [53] hour,[^n3612] and the sun became dark. [^n3613]And at the ninth hour Jesus cried out with a loud voice, and said, Yail, Yaili,[^n3614] why hast thou forsaken me? which[^n3615] is, My [54] God, my God, why hast thou forsaken me? [^n3616]And some of those that stood there, when they heard, said,[^n3617] This man called Elijah.

### Section LII

Section LII.

[1] [^n3618]And after that, Jesus knew that all things were finished; and that the scripture [2] might be accomplished, he said, I thirst. [^n3619]And there was set a vessel full of vinegar: and in that hour one of them hasted, and took a sponge, and filled it with that [3] vinegar, [^n3620]and fastened it on a reed, and brought it near[^n3621] his mouth to give him a [4] drink. [^n3622]And when Jesus had taken that vinegar, he said, Everything is finished. [5] [^n3623]But the rest said, Let be, that we may[^n3624] see whether Elijah cometh to save him. [6, 7] [^n3625]And Jesus said, My Father, forgive them; for they know not what they do. And Jesus cried again with a loud voice, and said, My Father, into thy hands I commend[^n3626] my spirit. [^n3627]He said that, and bowed his head, and gave up his spirit.

[8] [^n3628]And immediately the face of[^n3629] the door of the temple was rent into two parts from [9] top to bottom; [^n3630]and the earth was shaken; and the stones were split to pieces; and the [Arabic, p. 197] tombs were opened; and the bodies of many saints which slept, arose and [10] came forth; [^n3631]and after his resurrection they entered into the holy city and [11] appeared unto many. [^n3632]And the officer of the footsoldiers, and they that were with him who were guarding Jesus,[^n3633] when they saw the earthquake, and the things which came [12] to pass, feared greatly, and praised God, and said, [^n3634]This man was righteous; and, [13] Truly he was the Son of God. [^n3635]And all the multitudes that were come together to the sight, when they saw what came to pass, returned and smote upon their breasts.

[14] [^n3636]And the Jews, because of the Friday, said, Let these bodies not remain on their crosses,[^n3637] because it is the morning of the sabbath (for that sabbath was a great day); and they asked of Pilate that they might break the legs of those that were [15] crucified, and take them down. [^n3638]And the soldiers came, and brake the legs of the [16] first, and that other which was crucified with him: [^n3639]but when they came to Jesus, [17] they saw that he had died before, so they brake not his legs: [^n3640]but one of the soldiers pierced[^n3641] him in his side with a spear, and immediately there came forth blood and [18] water. [^n3642]And he that hath seen hath borne witness, and his witness is true: and he [19] knoweth that he hath said the truth, that ye also may believe. [^n3643]This he did, that [20] the scripture might be fulfilled, which saith, A bone shall not be broken in him; [^n3644]and the scripture also which saith, Let them look upon him whom they pierced.[^n3645]

[21] [^n3646]And there were in the distance all the acquaintance of Jesus standing, and the women that came with him from Galilee, those that followed him and ministered. [22] [^n3647]One of them was Mary Magdalene; and Mary the mother of James the little and [23] [Arabic, p. 198] Joses, [^n3648]and the mother of the sons of Zebedee, and Salome, and many others which came up with him unto Jerusalem; [^n3649]and they saw that.

[24] [^n3650]And when the evening of the Friday was come, because of the entering of the [25] sabbath, [^n3651]there came a rich man,[^n3652] a noble[^n3653] of Ramah,[^n3654] a city of Judah,[^n3655] named Joseph, and he was a good man and upright; [^n3656]and he was a[^n3657] disciple of Jesus, but [26] was concealing himself for fear of the Jews. [^n3658]And he did not agree with the accusers [27] in their desire and their deeds: [^n3659]and he was looking for the kingdom of God. [^n3660]And this man went boldly, and entered in unto Pilate, and asked of him the body of [28] Jesus. [^n3661]And Pilate wondered how he had died already: and he called the officer of [29] the footsoldiers, and asked him concerning his death before the time. [^n3662]And when [30] he knew, he commanded him to deliver up his body unto Joseph. [^n3663]And Joseph bought for him a winding cloth of pure linen, and took down the body of Jesus, [31] and wound it in it; and they came and took it. [^n3664]And there came unto him Nicodemus also, who of old came unto Jesus by night; and he brought with him perfume[^n3665] [32] of myrrh and aloes, about a hundred pounds. [^n3666]And they took the body of Jesus, and wound it in the linen and the perfume, as was the custom of the Jews to bury.

[33] [^n3667]And there was in the place where Jesus was crucified a garden; and in that garden [34] a new tomb cut out in a rock,[^n3668] wherein was never man yet laid. [^n3669]And they left [35] Jesus there because the sabbath had come in, and because the tomb was near. [^n3670]And they pushed[^n3671] a great stone, and thrust[^n3672] it against the door of the sepulchre, and [36] went away. [^n3673]And Mary Magdalene and Mary that was related to Joses came to [37] [Arabic, p. 199] the sepulchre after them,[^n3674] [^n3675]and sat opposite the sepulchre,[^n3676] and saw the [38] body, how they took it in and laid it there. [^n3677]And they returned, and bought ointment[^n3678] and perfume,[^n3679] and prepared[^n3680] it, that they might come and anoint him. [39] [^n3681]And on the day which was the sabbath day they desisted according to the command.

[40, 41] [^n3682]And the chief priests and the Pharisees gathered unto Pilate, and said unto him, [^n3683]Our lord, we remember that that misleader said, while he was alive, After three days [42] I rise. [^n3684]And now send beforehand and guard the tomb[^n3685] until the third day,[^n3686] lest his disciples come and steal him by night, and they will say unto the people that he [43] is risen from the dead: and the last error shall be worse than the first. [^n3687]He said unto them, And have ye not guards?[^n3688] go, and take precautions as ye know how. [44] [^n3689]And they went, and set guards at the tomb, and sealed that stone, with the guards.

[45] [^n3690]And in the evening of the sabbath, which is the morning of the first day, and in [46] the dawning[^n3691] while the darkness yet remained, came Mary Magdalene and the other Mary and other women to see the tomb. [^n3692]They brought with them the [47] perfume which they had prepared, and said among themselves, [^n3693]Who is it that will [48] remove for us the stone from the door of the tomb? for it was very great. [^n3694]And when they said thus, there occurred a great earthquake; and an angel came down [49] from heaven, and came and removed the stone from the door. [^n3695]And they came and found the stone removed from the sepulchre, and the angel sitting upon the [50] stone. [^n3696]And his appearance was as the lightning, and his raiment white as the [51] snow: [^n3697]and for fear of him the guards were troubled, and became as dead men. [52] [^n3698]And when he went away, the women entered into the sepulchre; and they found [53] [Arabic, p. 200] not the body of Jesus. [^n3699]And they saw there a young man sitting on the [54] right, arrayed in a white garment; and they were amazed.[^n3700] [^n3701]And the angel answered and said unto the women, Fear ye not: for I know that ye seek Jesus the [55] Nazarene, who hath been crucified. He is not here; but he is risen, as he said. [^n3702]Come and see the place where our Lord lay.

### Section LIII

Section LIII.[^n3703]

[1] [^n3704]And while they marvelled at that, behold, two men standing above them, their [2] raiment shining: [^n3705]and they were seized with fright, and bowed down their face to [3] the earth: and they said unto them, Why seek ye the living one with the dead? [^n3706]He is not here; he is risen: remember what he was speaking unto you while he was in [4] Galilee, and saying, [^n3707]The Son of man is to be delivered up into the hands of sinners, [5] and to be crucified, and on the third day to rise. [^n3708]But go in haste, and say to his disciples and to Cephas, He is risen from among the dead; and lo, he goeth before [6] you into Galilee; [^n3709]and there ye shall see him, where[^n3710] he said unto you: lo, I have [7] told you. [^n3711]And they remembered his sayings; and they departed in haste from the [8] tomb with joy and great fear, and hastened and went; [^n3712]and perplexity and fear [9] encompassed them; and they told no man anything, for they were afraid. [^n3713]And Mary hastened, and came to Simon Cephas, and to that other disciple whom Jesus loved, and said unto them, They have taken our Lord from the sepulchre, and I [10] know not where they have laid him. [^n3714]And Simon went out, and that other disciple, [11] and came to the sepulchre. [^n3715]And they hastened both together: and that disciple [12] outran[^n3716] Simon, and came first to the sepulchre; [^n3717]and he looked down, and saw the [13] linen laid; but he went not in. [^n3718]And Simon came after him, and entered into the [14] [Arabic, p. 201] sepulchre, and saw the linen laid; [^n3719]and the scarf with which his head was bound was not with the linen, but wrapped and laid aside in a certain place. [15] [^n3720]Then entered that disciple which came first to the sepulchre, and saw, and believed. [16] [^n3721]And they knew not yet from the scriptures that the Messiah was to rise from among [17] the dead. [^n3722]And those two disciples went to their place.

[18] [^n3723]But Mary remained[^n3724] at the tomb weeping: and while she wept, she looked [19] down into the tomb; [^n3725]and she saw two angels sitting in white raiment, one of them toward his pillow, and the other toward his feet, where the body of Jesus had been [20] laid. [^n3726]And they said unto her, Woman, why weepest thou? She said unto them, [21] They have taken my Lord, and I know not where they have left him. [^n3727]She said that, and turned behind her, and saw Jesus standing, and knew not that it was [22] Jesus. [^n3728]Jesus said unto her, Woman, why weepest thou? whom seekest thou? And she supposed[^n3729] him to be the gardener, and said, My lord, if thou hast taken him, [23] tell me where thou hast laid him, that I may go and take him. [^n3730]Jesus said unto her, Mary. She turned, and said unto him in Hebrew, Rabboni; which is, being [24] interpreted, Teacher. [^n3731]Jesus said unto her, Touch me not;[^n3732] for I have not ascended yet unto my Father: go to my brethren, and say unto them, I ascend unto my Father and your Father, and my God and your God.

[25] [^n3733]And on the First-day on which he rose, he appeared first unto Mary Magdalene, from whom he had cast out seven demons.

[26] [^n3734]And some of those guards[^n3735] came to the city, and informed the chief priests of [27] [Arabic, p. 202] all that had happened. [^n3736]And they assembled with the elders, and took [28] counsel; [^n3737]and they gave money, not a little, to the guards, and said unto them, Say ye, His disciples came and stole him by night, while we were sleeping.

[29] [^n3738]And if the judge hear that, we will make a plea with him, and free you of blame. [30] [^n3739]And they, when they took the money, did according to what they taught them. And this word spread among the Jews unto this day.

[31] [^n3740]And then came Mary Magdalene, and announced to the disciples that she had seen our Lord, and that he had said that unto her.

[32] [^n3741]And while the first[^n3742] women[^n3743] were going in the way to inform[^n3744] his disciples,[^n3745] [33] Jesus met them, and said unto them, Peace unto you. [^n3746]And they came and took [34] hold of his feet, and worshipped him. [^n3747]Then said Jesus unto them, Fear not: but go and say to my brethren that they depart into Galilee, and there they shall see [35] me. [^n3748]And those women returned, and told all that to the eleven, and to the rest of the disciples; [^n3749]and to those that had been with him, for they were saddened and [36] weeping. [^n3750]And those were Mary Magdalene, and Joanna, and Mary the mother of James, and the rest who were with them: and they were those that told the apostles. [37] [^n3751]And they, when they heard them say that he was alive and had appeared unto them, [38] did not believe them: [^n3752]and these sayings were before their eyes as the sayings of madness. [39] [Arabic, p. 203] [^n3753]And after that, he appeared to two of them, on that day, and while they were going to the village which was named Emmaus, and whose distance [40] from Jerusalem was sixty furlongs.[^n3754] [^n3755]And they were talking the one of them with the [41] other of all the things which had happened. [^n3756]And during the time of their talking and [42] inquiring with one another, Jesus came and reached them, and walked with them. [^n3757]But [43] their eyes were veiled that they should not know him. [^n3758]And he said unto them, What are these sayings which ye address the one of you to the other, as ye walk and are [44] sad? [^n3759]One of them, whose name was Cleopas, answered and said unto him, Art thou perchance alone a stranger to Jerusalem, since thou knowest not what was in [45] it in these days? He said unto them, What was? [^n3760]They said unto him, Concerning Jesus, he who was from Nazareth, a man who was a prophet, and powerful in [46] speech and deeds before God and before all the people: [^n3761]and the chief priests and [47] the elders delivered him up to the sentence of[^n3762] death, and crucified him. [^n3763]But we supposed that he was the one who was to deliver Israel. And since all[^n3764] these [48] things happened there have passed three days. [^n3765]But certain women of us also [49] informed us that they had come to the sepulchre; [^n3766]and when they found not his body, they came and told us that they had seen there the angels, and they[^n3767] said [50] concerning him that he was alive. [^n3768]And some of us also went to the sepulchre, and [51] found the matter as the women had said: only they saw him not. [^n3769]Then said Jesus [52] unto them, Ye lacking in discernment, and heavy in heart to believe! [^n3770]Was it not in all the sayings of the prophets that the Messiah was to suffer these things, and to [53] [Arabic, p. 204] enter into his Glory? [^n3771]And he began from Moses and from all the prophets, [54] and interpreted to them concerning himself from all the scriptures. [^n3772]And they drew near unto the village, whither they were going: and he was leading them to [55] imagine that he was as if going to a distant region. [^n3773]And they pressed[^n3774] him, and said unto him, Abide with us: for the day hath declined now to the darkness. And he went [56] in to abide with them. [^n3775]And when he sat with them, he took bread, and blessed, [57] and brake, and gave to them. [^n3776]And straightway their eyes were opened, and they [58] knew him; and he was taken away from them.[^n3777] [^n3778]And they said the one to the other, Was not our heart heavy within us, while he was speaking to us in the way, and interpreting to us the scriptures?

[59] [^n3779]And they rose in that hour, and returned to Jerusalem, and found the eleven [60] gathered, and those that were with them, saying, [^n3780]Truly our Lord is risen, and hath [61] appeared to Simon. [^n3781]And they related what happened in the way, and how they knew him when he brake the bread. [^n3782]Neither believed they that also.

### Section LIV

Section LIV.

[1] [^n3783]And while they talked together,[^n3784] the evening of that day arrived which was the First-day; and the doors were shut where the disciples were, because of the fear of the [2] Jews; [^n3785]and Jesus came and stood among them, and said unto them, Peace be with you: I am he; fear not. [^n3786]But they were agitated, and became afraid, and supposed that they [3] saw a spirit. [^n3787]Jesus said unto them, Why are ye agitated? and why do thoughts rise [4] [Arabic, p. 205] in[^n3788] your hearts? [^n3789]See my hands and my feet, that I am he: feel me, and know that a spirit hath not flesh and bones, as ye see me having that. [5] [^n3790]And when he had said this, he shewed them his hands and his feet and his side.[^n3791] [6] [^n3792]And they were until this time unbelieving, from their joy and their wonder. He [7] said unto them, Have ye anything here to eat? [^n3793]And they gave him a portion of broiled fish and of honey.[^n3794] [^n3795]And he took it, and ate before them.

[8] [^n3796]And he said unto them, These are the sayings which I spake unto you, while I was with you, that[^n3797] everything must be fulfilled, which is written in the law of [9] Moses, and the prophets, and the psalms, concerning me. [^n3798]Then opened he their [10] heart, that they might understand the scriptures; and he said unto them, [^n3799]Thus it is written, and thus it is necessary[^n3800] that the Messiah suffer, and rise from among the [11] dead on the third day; [^n3801]and that repentance unto the forgiveness of sins be preached [12] in his name among all the peoples; and the beginning shall be from Jerusalem. [^n3802]And [13] ye shall be witnesses of that. And I send unto you the promise of my Father. [^n3803]And [14] when the disciples heard that, they were glad. [^n3804]And Jesus said unto them again, [15] Peace be with you: as my Father hath sent me, I also send you. [^n3805]And when he had said this, he breathed on them, and said unto them, Receive ye the Holy Spirit: [16] [^n3806]and if ye forgive sins to any man, they shall be forgiven him; and if ye retain them against any man, they shall be retained.

[17] [^n3807]But Thomas, one of the twelve, called Thama, was not there with the disciples [18] when Jesus came. [^n3808]The disciples therefore said unto him, We have seen our Lord. But he said unto them, If I do not see in his hands the places of the nails, and put on them my fingers, and pass my hand over his side, I will not believe.

[19] [^n3809]And after eight days, on the next First-day, the disciples were assembled again within, and Thomas with them. And Jesus came, the doors being shut, and stood [20] [Arabic, p. 206] in the midst, and said unto them, Peace be with you. [^n3810]And he said to Thomas, Bring hither thy finger, and behold my hands; and bring hither [21] thy hand, and spread it on my side: and be not unbelieving, but believing. [^n3811]Thomas [22] answered and said unto him, My Lord and my God. [^n3812]Jesus said unto him, Now since thou hast seen me, thou hast believed: blessed are they that have not seen me, and have believed.

[23] [^n3813]And many other signs did Jesus before his disciples, and they are they which [24] are not written in this book: [^n3814]but these that[^n3815] are written also are that ye may believe in Jesus the Messiah, the Son of God; and that when ye have believed, ye may have in his name eternal life.

[25] [^n3816]And after that, Jesus shewed himself again to his disciples at the sea of Tiberias; [26] and he shewed himself unto them thus. [^n3817]And there were together Simon Cephas, and Thomas which was called Twin,[^n3818] and Nathanael who was of Cana of Galilee, [27] and the sons of Zebedee, and two other of the disciples. [^n3819]Simon Cephas said unto them, I go to catch fish. They said unto him, And we also come with thee. And they went forth, and went up into the boat; and in that night they caught nothing. [28] [^n3820]And when the morning arrived, Jesus stood on the shore of the sea: but the disciples [29] knew not that it was Jesus. [^n3821]And Jesus said unto them, Children, have ye anything [30] to eat? They said unto him, No. [^n3822]He said unto them, Cast your net from the right side of the boat, and ye shall find.[^n3823] And they threw, and they were not able [31] to draw the net for the abundance of the fish that were come[^n3824] into it. [^n3825]And that disciple whom Jesus loved said to Cephas, This is our Lord. And Simon, when he heard that it was our Lord, took his tunic, and girded it on his waist (for he was [32] naked), and cast himself into the sea to come to Jesus. [^n3826]But some others of the disciples came in the boat[^n3827] (and they were not far from the land, but about two [33] [Arabic, p. 207] hundred cubits), and drew that net of fish. [^n3828]And when they went up on the [34] land, they saw live coals laid, and fish laid thereon, and bread. [^n3829]And Jesus [35] said unto them, Bring of this fish which ye have now caught. [^n3830]Simon Cephas therefore went up, and dragged the net to the land, full of great fish, a hundred and fifty-three [36] fishes: and with all this weight that net was not rent. [^n3831]And Jesus said unto them, Come and sit down. And no man of the disciples dared to ask him who he was, for they knew that it was our Lord. But he did not appear to them in his own [37, 38] form. [^n3832]And Jesus came, and took bread and fish, and gave unto them. [^n3833]This is the third time that Jesus appeared to his disciples, when he had risen from among the dead.

[39] [^n3834]And when they had breakfasted, Jesus said to Simon Cephas, Simon, son of Jonah, lovest thou me more than these? He said unto him, Yea, my Lord; thou [40] knowest that I love thee. [^n3835]Jesus said unto him, Feed for me my lambs. He said unto him again a second time, Simon, son of Jonah, lovest thou me? He said unto him, Yea, my Lord; thou knowest that I love thee. He said unto him, Feed for [41] me my sheep.[^n3836] [^n3837]He said unto him again the third time, Simon, son of Jonah, lovest thou me? And it grieved Cephas that he said unto him three times, Lovest thou me? He said unto him, My Lord, thou knowest everything; thou knowest that I [42] love thee. [^n3838]Jesus said unto him, Feed for me my sheep.[^n3839] Verily, verily, I say unto thee, When thou wast a child, thou didst gird thy waist for thyself, and go whither [Arabic, p. 208] thou wouldest: but when thou shalt be old, thou shalt stretch out thy hands, and another shall gird thy waist, and take thee whither thou wouldest not. [43] [^n3840]He said that to him to explain by what death he was to glorify God. And when he [44] had said that, he said unto him, Come after me. [^n3841]And Simon Cephas turned, and saw that disciple whom Jesus loved following him; he which at the supper leaned[^n3842] on [45] Jesus’ breast, and said, My Lord, who is it that betrayeth thee? [^n3843]When therefore Cephas saw him, he said to Jesus, My Lord, and this man, what shall be in his [46] case?[^n3844] [^n3845]Jesus said unto him, If I will that this man remain until I come, what is [47] that to thee? follow thou me. [^n3846]And this word spread among the brethren, that that disciple should not die: but Jesus said not that he should not die; but, If I will that this man remain until I come, what is that to thee?

[48] [^n3847]This is the disciple which bare witness of that, and wrote it: and we know that his witness is true.

### Section LV

Section LV.

[1] [^n3848]But the eleven disciples went into Galilee, to the mountain[^n3849] where Jesus had [2] appointed them. [^n3850]And when they saw him, they worshipped him: but there were of [3] them who doubted. [^n3851]And while they sat there he appeared to them again, and upbraided them for their lack of faith and the hardness of their hearts, those that saw him when he was risen, and believed not.[^n3852]

[4] [Arabic, p. 209] [^n3853]Then said Jesus unto them, I have been given all authority in heaven [5] and earth; and as my Father hath sent me, so I also send you. [^n3854]Go now into [6] all the world, and preach my gospel in all the creation; [^n3855]and teach[^n3856] all the peoples, and [7] baptize them in the name of the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit; [^n3857]and teach them to keep all whatsoever I commanded you: and lo, I am with you all the days, unto [8] the end of the world. [^n3858]For whosoever believeth and is baptized shall be saved; but [9] whosoever believeth not shall be rejected. [^n3859]And the signs[^n3860] which shall attend those that believe in me are these: that they shall cast out devils in my name; and they [10] shall speak with new tongues; [^n3861]and they shall take up serpents, and if they drink deadly poison,[^n3862] it shall not injure them; and they shall lay their hands on the diseased, [11] and they shall be healed. [^n3863]But ye, abide in the city of Jerusalem, until ye be clothed with power from on high.

[12] [^n3864]And our Lord Jesus, after speaking to them, took them out to Bethany: and he [13] lifted up his hands, and blessed them. [^n3865]And while he blessed them, he was separated from them, and ascended into heaven, and sat down at the right hand of God. [14, 15] [^n3866]And they worshipped him, and returned to Jerusalem with great joy: [^n3867]and at all times they were in the temple, praising and blessing God. Amen.

[16] [^n3868]And from thence they went forth, and preached in every place; and our Lord helped them, and confirmed their sayings by the signs which they did.[^n3869]

[17] [^n3870]And here are also many other things which Jesus did, which if they were written every one of them, not even the world, according to my opinion, would contain the books which should be written.[^n3871]

### Subscriptions.

Subscriptions.

I. In Borgian ms.

Here endeth the Gospel which Tatianus compiled and named Diatessaron, i.e., The Fourfold, a compilation from the four Gospels of the holy Apostles, the excellent Evangelists (peace be upon them). It was translated by the excellent and learned priest, Abu’l Faraj ‘Abdulla ibn-at-Tayyib[^n3872] (may God grant him favour), from Syriac into Arabic, from an exemplar written by ‘Isa[^n3873] ibn-‘Ali al-Motatabbib,[^n3874] pupil of Honain ibn-Ishak (God have mercy on them both). Amen.

2. In Vatican ms.[^n3875]

Here endeth, by the help of God, the holy Gospel that Titianus compiled from the four Gospels, which is known as Diatessaron. And praise be to God, as he is entitled to it and lord of it! And to him be the glory for ever.

[^n173]: Matt. i. 18.

[^n174]: This is preceded in Vat. ms. by the genealogy, Matt. i. 1–17 (see Introduction, 13), with the marginal note The Beginning of the Gospel of Matthew. (Lagarde, op. cit., 1886, p. 154.) The text presents nothing worthy of note in this place except that verse 16, construed on the same principle as the preceding verses, to which, except in the words printed in italics, it is strictly parallel in construction, reads thus: “Jacob begat Joseph, the husband of Mary, who of her begat Jesus, the Messiah” (cf. the remarkable reading of Sin. Syriac). As it stands, this is the only possible interpretation of the words, for who is masculine. But a mistake in the gender of a relative pronoun is very common in Arabic among illiterate people, while in Syriac there is, to begin with, no distinction. If then we correct the relative, who of her will become of whom (fem.), and begat will of course be construed as passive. We thus get the text followed in Ibn-at-Tayyib’s Commentary, the ordinary reading of the Peshitta, of whom was born Jesus.

Here the Borg. ms. has of her for in her; but Ibn-at-Tayyib in his Commentary discusses why Matthew wrote in and not of.

[^n3871]: In the Borg. ms. the text ends on folio 353a. On folios 354a-355a are found the genealogies, with the title, Book of the Generation of Jesus, that of Luke following that of Matthew without any break. Ciasca has told us nothing of the nature of the text. The Subscription follows on folio 355b.
